An arithmetic étale-crystalline comparison with coefficients in crystalline local systems
Maximilian Hauck

Abstract
. We use the stacky approach to p p -adic cohomology theories recently developed by Drinfeld and Bhatt–Lurie to generalise a comparison theorem between the rational crystalline cohomology of the special fibre and the rational p p -adic étale cohomology of the arithmetic generic fibre of any proper p p -adic formal scheme X X due to Colmez–Nizioł to the case of coefficients in an arbitrary crystalline local system on the generic fibre of X X . In the process, we establish a version of the Beilinson fibre square of Antieau–Mathew–Morrow–Nikolaus with coefficients in the proper case and prove a comparison between syntomic cohomology and p p -adic étale cohomology with coefficients in an arbitrary F F -gauge. Our methods also yield a description of the isogeny category of perfect F F -gauges on Z p \mathbb{Z}_{p} .



[intoc]

1.
Introduction

While the idea that one may compute the value of a cohomology theory attached to a scheme
X
by instead computing the coherent cohomology of a suitably defined stack attached to
X
goes back, in the case of de Rham cohomology, to work of Simpson in the 1990s, see
[
39
]
and
[
40
]
, this approach has only recently found entrance into the field of
p
-adic Hodge theory and has been starting to be fully exploited in the course of the last few years with the formulation of prismatic cohomology in terms of stacks independently developed by Bhatt–Lurie and Drinfeld in
[
7
]
,
[
8
]
,
[
6
]
and
[
18
]
. Roughly speaking,
similarly to how one can attach to any
p
-adic formal scheme
X
its
de Rham stack

X^{\mathrm{dR}}
, which has the property that coherent cohomology of the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{dR}}}
agrees with the (
p
-completed) de Rham cohomology of
X
if
X
is smooth,
they functorially attach a stack
X{\Delta}
to any
such

X
with the feature that coherent cohomology of the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X{\Delta}}
agrees with the (absolute) prismatic cohomology of
X

if
X
is
p
-quasisyntomic in the sense of
[
7
, Def. C.6]
; correspondingly, the stack
X{\Delta}
is called the
prismatisation
of
X
. Moreover, similarly to how the de Rham stack
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
admits a filtered refinement
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
computing the Hodge filtration, they also introduce a filtered refinement
X^{\mathrm{N}}
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
of
X{\Delta}
computing the (absolute) Nygaard filtration on prismatic cohomology. Finally, there is an even more refined variant
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
called the
syntomification
of
X
, which arises by gluing together two copies of
X{\Delta}
which sit inside
X^{\mathrm{N}}
as open substacks.

The upshot of this picture is twofold: First, various statements about prismatic cohomology and related cohomology theories now admit a “geometric” formulation;
for example
, the comparison between prismatic cohomology and de Rham cohomology from
[
7
, Thm. 5.4.2]
can be reinterpreted as saying that, for any smooth
p
-adic formal scheme
X
, there is a functorial isomorphism

(X_{p=0}){\Delta}\cong X^{\mathrm{dR}}\;.

Second, the stacky formulation immediately furnishes natural categories of coefficients for the respective cohomology theories: these should just be the categories of quasi-coherent complexes, or perhaps perfect complexes, on the corresponding stack.

For us, the most important such category of will be the category of
F
-gauges
on
X
defined by

\FGauge{\Delta}(X)\coloneqq\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\;.

The fact that (perfect)
F
-gauges play a central role in the theory is due to the fact that they seem to be some sort of “universal coefficients” for
p
-adic cohomology theories,
i.e. they appear to be good candidates for a theory of
p
-adic motives
: namely, they admit various realisation functors towards more classical notions of coefficients for
p
-adic cohomology theories, e.g. a
de Rham realisation

T_{\mathrm{dR}}:\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR}})\cong\Vect^{\nabla}(X)

towards the category of vector bundles on
X
equipped with a flat connection; a filtered refinement

T_{\mathrm{dR},+}:\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\;,

where the right-hand side may roughly be described as filtered vector bundles on
X
equipped with a flat connection satisfying Griffiths transversality,
see
Remark

2.2.12
; an
étale realisation

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}:\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})

towards the category of pro-étale
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local systems on the generic fibre
X_{\eta}
of
X
;
and a
crystalline realisation

T_{\mathrm{crys}}:\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\Vect((X_{p=0})^{\mathrm{Syn}})\;,

which may be postcomposed with a natural functor

\Vect((X_{p=0})^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\Isoc^{\varphi}(X_{p=0}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})

towards the category of
F
-isocrystals on the special fibre
X_{p=0}
of
X
.
The details of this picture as well as the necessary background on the stacks
X^{\mathrm{dR}},X{\Delta}
, etc. will be reviewed in Section
Section

2
.

In this paper, we make use of these features of the stacky approach to
p
-adic cohomology theories and, in particular, syntomic cohomology to prove the following comparison theorem between the crystalline cohomology of the special fibre of a smooth proper
p
-adic formal scheme
X
and the étale cohomology of its generic fibre. For the relevant definitions about crystalline local systems, we refer to Section
5.1
.

Theorem 1.0.1

(
Theorem

5.0.1
)
.

Let
X
be a
p
-adic formal scheme
which is smooth and proper over
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. For any crystalline local system
L
on
X_{\eta}
with Hodge–Tate weights all at most
-i-1
for some
i\geq 0
, let
\mathcal{E}
be its associated
F
-isocrystal. Then there is a natural morphism

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{crys}}(X_{p=0},\mathcal{E})[\tfrac{1}{p}][-1]\rightarrow R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},L)[\tfrac{1}{p}]\;

which induces an isomorphism

\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{crys}}(X_{p=0},\mathcal{E})[\tfrac{1}{p}][-1]\cong\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},L)[\tfrac{1}{p}]

and an injection on
H^{i+1}
.

This generalises a previous result of Colmez–Nizioł who obtained essentially the same statement in the special case of
L=\mathbb{Z}_{p}(n)
being a Tate twist, see
[
15
, Cor. 1.4]
. Our strategy to prove this will be to first construct an
F
-gauge corresponding to any crystalline
local system
building on the works
[
26
]
of Guo–Reinecke and
[
24
]
of Guo–Li; more precisely, in Section
5.2
, we prove that:

Theorem 1.0.2

(
Theorem

5.2.7
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme. Then there is a fully faithful embedding

\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})\hookrightarrow\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})

which is a weak right inverse to étale realisation. The image of a crystalline local system
L
under this embedding is called the
associated
F
-gauge

E
of
L
and has the property that
T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E)
identifies with the associated
F
-isocrystal of
L
.

Then the proof is divided into two parts: On the one hand, we show that, in the range which is relevant to us, étale cohomology with coefficients in a crystalline local system agrees with syntomic cohomology with coefficients in the associated
F
-gauge. More precisely, in Section
4
, we obtain the following theorem:

Theorem 1.0.3

(
Theorem

4.0.2
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth qcqs
p
-adic formal scheme. For any vector bundle
F
-gauge
E\in\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
with Hodge–Tate weights all at most
-i-1
for some
i\geq 0
, the natural morphism

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},E)\rightarrow R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(E))

induces an isomorphism

\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},E)\cong\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(E))

and an injection on
H^{i+1}
.

On the other hand, in order to make the connection between syntomic cohomology and crystalline cohomology, we use Section
3
to prove the following version of the Beilinson fibre square from
[
3
]
with coefficients, which recovers the previously known result
[
3
, Thm. 6.17]
of Antieau–Mathew–Morrow–Nikolaus in the case of smooth and proper
p
-adic formal schemes upon plugging in a Breuil–Kisin twist
\mathcal{O}\{n\}
for the
F
-gauge
E
:

Theorem 1.0.4

(
Corollary

3.0.3
)
.

Let
X
be a
p
-adic formal scheme which is smooth and proper over
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. For any perfect
F
-gauge
E\in\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
, there is a natural pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},E)[\frac{1}{p}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma((X_{p=0})^{\mathrm{Syn}},T_{\mathrm{crys}}(E))[\frac{1}{p}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},+},T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E))[\frac{1}{p}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR}},T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E))[\frac{1}{p}]}

in the category
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

We note here that this really also gives a new proof of the special case
E=\mathcal{O}\{n\}
treated in
[
3
, Thm. 6.17]
: Indeed, while our argument uses results from
[
26
]
, which in turn are proved using the Beilinson fibre square in the form from
[
3
, Thm. 6.17]
, Du–Liu–Moon–Shimizu have shown in
[
17
]
that one can also obtain the same results via a different route which does not make use of the Beilinson fibre square, see also
[
26
, Rem. 1.9]
. In particular, in contrast to the proof of Antieau–Mathew–Morrow–Nikolaus, our proof does not make use of topological Hochschild homology and only relies on methods from the theory of prismatic cohomology. Let us also remark here that,
during our work on the contents of this paper, we learnt that,
in currently still unpublished work, Lurie has recently found yet another proof of the Beilinson fibre square, which also makes use of the stacky formulation of syntomic cohomology, but still significantly differs from our proof. In particular, his proof should allow one to drop the properness hypothesis in the above result and one can then also remove the properness hypothesis from our main theorem.

Moreover, we also show that the pullback square from
Theorem

1.0.4
actually comes from a commutative square of stacks

(1.0.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

which gives a new perspective on the Beilinson fibre square as a statement about the geometry of the stack
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
: Indeed, one may reinterpret
Theorem

1.0.4
as saying that, at least from the perspective of cohomology after inverting
p
, the stack
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}

behaves like it
is glued together from the stacks
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+}
. Indeed, we also prove that this interpretation is reflected on the level of derived categories:

Theorem 1.0.5

(
Theorem

3.6.1
)
.

The functor

(1.0.2)

\Perf(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}})[\tfrac{1}{p}]\rightarrow\mathcal{DF}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times_{\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\mathcal{D}(\Mod^{\varphi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))

induced by the diagram (
1.0.1
) is a fully faithful embedding. Here,
\mathcal{DF}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denotes the filtered derived category of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector spaces and
\Mod^{\varphi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is the category of
\varphi
-modules over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

In particular, note that the above is a categorical generalisation of
Theorem

1.0.4
: indeed, for any
E\in\Perf(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}})
, one recovers the statement of
Theorem

1.0.4
by computing
\RHom(\mathcal{O},E)[\tfrac{1}{p}]
via the right-hand side of (
1.0.2
).

Notations and Conventions

We freely make use of the language of
\infty
-categories in the style of Lurie, see
[
31
]
, and of the theory of derived algebraic geometry as laid out in
[
30
]
. In particular, we work derived throughout: e.g., all our pullbacks and pushforwards are in the derived sense, i.e. when we write
f_{*}
for a map
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
of schemes/formal schemes/stacks, we really mean the derived pushforward
Rf_{*}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})
.

All the stacks occurring in this paper are going to be in the fpqc topology on commutative rings and we point out that, for the purposes of this paper, it does not make a difference whether one works in the setting of stacks in groupoids or stacks in
\infty
-groupoids. By a quasi-coherent complex on a stack
\mathcal{X}
, we mean an object of the derived category
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
, which is defined via Kan extension from the affine case as in
[
30
, §3.2]
; the same applies to the full subcategories
\Vect(\mathcal{X})
and
\Perf(\mathcal{X})
of vector bundles and perfect complexes, respectively.

When we speak of “completeness” of a module or complex with respect to some ideal, we will usually mean derived completeness as defined, for example, in
[
41
, Tag 091N]
and distinguish any other usage of the term “complete” by speaking about “classical completeness”.
For a ring
A
and an ideal
I
,
we will use
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(A)
to denote the category of derived
I
-complete complexes of
A
-modules;
here, we omit the ideal
I
from the notation as it will generally be clear from the context.

Moreover, in the aforementioned situation, we denote the derived
I
-completion of a complex
M
of
A
-modules by
M_{I}^{\wedge}
.

We frequently make use of the Rees equivalence between the category of quasi-coherent complexes on
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
over a ring
R
and the category
\mathcal{DF}(R)
of filtered objects in
\mathcal{D}(R)
, see
[
6
, §2.2.1]
or
[
35
]
for an introduction to the Rees equivalence. However, beware that our sign convention slightly differs from the one in
[
6
]
: we use the stack
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, where the subscript
(-)_{-}
indicates that
\mathbb{G}_{m}
actson
\mathbb{A}^{1}
by placing the coordinate
t
on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
in grading degree
-1
, to realise the Rees equivalence for decreasing filtrations and the universal generalised Cartier divisor on
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is denoted
t:\mathcal{O}(1)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}
; this has the pleasant effect of removing the change of sign in the passage to the associated graded in
[
6
, Prop. 2.2.6.(3)]
. Similarly, the notation
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
indicates the quotient of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
by the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action given by placing the coordinate on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, which will be called
u
this time, in grading degree
1
and the universal section of
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is denoted
u:\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(1)
; in our sign convention, sheaves on
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
correspond to increasing filtrations.

From time to time, we need base change statements for cartesian squares of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{?}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{??}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{?}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{??}}

for
?,??\in\{\mathrm{dR},\mathbbl{\Delta},\mathrm{N},\dots\}
which are induced by a map
X\rightarrow Y
of formal schemes. We will usually use these without further justification and refer to Appendix
A
for details regarding how to prove such results.

Throughout,
p
is a fixed prime and
X
denotes a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme. If
X=\Spf R
is affine, we also use the notation
R^{\mathrm{dR}},R{\Delta},\dots
to denote the stacks
(\Spf R)^{\mathrm{dR}},(\Spf R){\Delta},\dots
.

We also fix an algebraic closure
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
whose completion we will denote by
C
.

2.
Recollections on stacks and
p
-adic cohomology theories

We briefly remind the reader of the essential input from
[
18
]
,
[
7
]
,
[
8
]
and
[
6
]
needed for our purposes. For a more thorough introduction, we advise the reader to consult these sources directly. Throughout, the idea will be that, given a cohomology theory
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{typ}}(-)
for
p
-adic formal schemes
X
, we will functorially attach a stack
X^{\mathrm{typ}}
to
X
with the property that, in good cases, coherent cohomology on the stack
X^{\mathrm{typ}}
computes
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{typ}}(X)
, i.e.

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{typ}},\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{typ}}})\cong R\Gamma_{\mathrm{typ}}(X)\;.

2.1.
Preliminaries on stacks

Before we can start talking about cohomology theories, let us make a few preliminary remarks about stacks in general.

2.1.1.
Formal stacks

We will almost exclusively deal with
(
p
-adic) formal stacks
, i.e. stacks
\mathcal{X}
such that
\mathcal{X}(S)
is only nonempty if
S
is
p
-nilpotent. Note that any adic ring
R
with an ideal of definition
I\subseteq R
containing
p
defines a formal stack
\Spf R
via
\Spf R\coloneqq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{n}\Spec R/I^{n}
and, if
R
is classically
I
-complete, then
(\Spf R)(S)
identifies with the groupoid of continuous morphisms from
R
to the discrete ring
S
. Clearly, any stack
\mathcal{X}
yields a formal stack
\mathcal{X}\times\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
; as we will soon work with formal stacks exclusively, we will almost always suppress the product with
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
from the notation and restrict to
p
-nilpotent rings
S
as our test objects.

In this setting of formal stacks, we make the following slightly nonstandard definition:

Definition 2.1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a property of formal schemes which is fpqc-local. Then we say that a formal stack
\mathcal{X}
has property
\mathcal{P}
if there is an fpqc cover from an affine formal scheme
\Spf R\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
such that
\Spf R
has property
\mathcal{P}
.

We remark that, for a ring
R
with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion endowed with the
p
-adic topology, the category
\mathcal{D}(\Spf R)
identifies with the category
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(R)
of derived
p
-complete objects in
\mathcal{D}(R)
, see
[
2
, Prop. A.11]
. Moreover, with the following definition, we can also recover the usual
t
-structure on
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(R)
by
[
2
, Ex. A.18]
:

Definition 2.1.2
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a stack. An object
F\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
is called
connective
if
f^{*}F\in\mathcal{D}(S)
is connective with respect to the standard
t
-structure for all
f:\Spec S\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
. The full subcategory of
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
spanned by connective objects is denoted
\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}(\mathcal{X})
.

Remark 2.1.3
.

Using
[
29
, §1]
, one can check that
\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}(\mathcal{X})
indeed defines the connective part of a
t
-structure
(\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}(\mathcal{X}),\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{X}))
on the category
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
.

The Rees equivalence also applies to our context of stacks over
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(instead of over
\Spec R
for some ring
R
). Indeed, one can show:

Lemma 2.1.4
.

For any stack
\mathcal{X}
, there are symmetric monoidal equivalences

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathcal{X})&\cong\mathcal{D}_{\gr}(\mathcal{X})\coloneqq\Fun(\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X}))\\
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathcal{X})&\cong\mathcal{DF}(\mathcal{X})\coloneqq\Fun((\mathbb{Z},\geq),\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X}))\;.\end{split}

Proof.

We only prove the first equivalence, the second one is similar. As any morphism
\Spec S\rightarrow B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathcal{X}
factors as
\Spec S\rightarrow B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\Spec S\rightarrow B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathcal{X}
, we obtain equivalences

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathcal{X})&\cong\lim_{\Spec S\rightarrow\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{D}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\Spec S)\cong\lim_{\Spec S\rightarrow\mathcal{X}}\Fun(\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{D}(S))\\
&\cong\Fun(\mathbb{Z},\lim_{\Spec S\rightarrow\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{D}(S))\cong\Fun(\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X}))\end{split}

using the Rees equivalence over the base
\Spec S
and the universal property of the limit.
∎

In particular, we see that
\mathcal{D}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p})
identify with the categories
\widehat{\mathcal{DF}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
of filtered objects and
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\gr}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
of graded objects in
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, respectively.

2.1.2.
Vector bundles and PD hulls

For a projective
R
-module
E
of finite rank, we adopt the convention that the geometric vector bundle
\mathbb{V}(E)\rightarrow\Spec R
corresponding to
E
is given by
\mathbb{V}(E)\coloneqq\Spec\Sym^{\bullet}_{R}E^{\vee}
. More generally, for a stack
\mathcal{X}
and a vector bundle
E\in\Vect(\mathcal{X})
, we define
\mathbb{V}(E)
by

\mathbb{V}(E)(S)=\{\text{pairs $f:\Spec S\rightarrow\mathcal{X},f^{*}E^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\Spec S})$}\}^{\cong}\;.

Particularly in the context of de Rham stacks, we will often consider the PD hull
\mathbb{V}(E)^{\sharp}
of the zero section of
\mathbb{V}(E)
. On points, this is given by

\begin{split}\mathbb{V}(E)^{\sharp}(S)=\{&\text{tuples $(f:\Spec S\rightarrow\mathcal{X},s_{n}:f^{*}E^{\otimes n\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\Spec S})$}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\text{such that $(s_{n})_{n}$ is a system of divided powers for $s_{1}$}\}^{\cong}\;.\end{split}

Here, the tuple
(s_{n})_{n}
being a system of divided powers for
s_{1}
means that the relations

(2.1.1)

s_{m}s_{n}=\binom{m+n}{n}s_{m+n}

are satisfied for all
m,n\geq 1
.

Example 2.1.5
.

The stack
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}
classifies ring elements equipped with a system of divided powers and is given by
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}=\Spec\mathbb{Z}[t,\tfrac{t^{2}}{2!},\tfrac{t^{3}}{3!},\dots]
.
∎

2.2.
De Rham stacks

Now we are in the position to define the de Rham stack. From now on, let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme; here,
X
being bounded means that it locally has the form
\Spf R
for
R
having bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion. We work with
p
-adic formal stacks
.

Definition 2.2.1
.

Consider the stack

\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}\coloneqq\Cone(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{can}}\mathbb{G}_{a})\;,

which admits the structure of a
1
-truncated animated
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-algebra stack. The
de Rham stack

X^{\mathrm{dR}}
of
X
is the stack defined by

X^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)\coloneqq\Map(\Spec\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S),X)\;,

where the mapping space is computed in derived algebraic geometry.

Example 2.2.2
.

Let
R
be a perfectoid ring and denote by
(A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R),I)=({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R},I)
the initial object of the absolute prismatic site of
R
; i.e.
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)=W(R^{\flat})
, where, as usual,

R^{\flat}\coloneqq\lim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}R\cong\lim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}R/p

and
I
is the kernel of the Fontaine map

\theta:A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)\rightarrow R\;,\hskip 14.22636pt\sum_{k}p^{k}[x_{k}]\mapsto\sum_{k}p^{k}x_{k}^{\sharp}\;;

here, for
x\in R^{\flat}=\lim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}R
, we denote by
x^{\sharp}
the image of
x
under the projection onto the first coordinate.
As
I
is principal, we may pick a generator
\xi\in A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)
and let

A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R)\coloneqq A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)[\tfrac{\xi^{n}}{n!}:n\geq 0]_{(p)}^{\wedge}

be the
p
-completed divided power envelope of
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)
at
\xi
; more canonically, one may describe
A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R)
as the
p
-completed divided power envelope of
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)
at
I
. Let us emphasise that we are using the free divided power envelope here, i.e.
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)[\tfrac{\xi^{n}}{n!}:n\geq 0]
denotes the quotient of the polynomial ring
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)[s_{1},s_{2},\dots]
by the relations
s_{1}=\xi
and (
2.1.1
). This is not the same as the subring of
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)[\tfrac{1}{p}]
generated by the elements
\tfrac{\xi^{n}}{n!}
for
n\geq 0
: e.g., for
R=k
a perfect field of characteristic field, we have

A_{\mathrm{crys}}(k)\cong(W(k)[x_{1},x_{2},\dots]/(px_{1}-p^{p},px_{2}-x_{1}^{p},\dots))^{\vee}_{(p)}

even though
d=p
already admits divided powers in
W(k)
!

We claim that

(2.2.1)

R^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong\Spf A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R)\;,

where
A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R)
is endowed with the
(p,\xi)
-adic topology (note that this agrees with the
p
-adic topology due to the existence of divided powers for
\xi
).
Indeed, we may describe this isomorphism on
S
-valued points for
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}
-acyclic
p
-nilpotent rings
S
by
Lemma

2.2.3
below. Then
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)=\Cone(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}(S)\rightarrow S)
and an
S
-valued point of
R^{\mathrm{dR}}
is the datum of a morphism
R\rightarrow\Cone(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}(S)\rightarrow S)
. We will shortly show that this yields a contractibly unique lift

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R=A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)/I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cone(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}(S)\rightarrow S)\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

The commutativity of the diagram then provides a system of divided powers for the image of
I
in
S
and hence induces a unique map
A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R)\rightarrow S
; i.e. we obtain an
S
-valued point of
\Spf A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R)
.
Finally, one easily sees that the construction is reversible: an
S
-valued point of
\Spf A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R)
furnishes a composite map
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)\rightarrow A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R)\rightarrow S
together with a system of divided powers for the image of
I
in
S
and hence a factorisation of the composite
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)\rightarrow S\rightarrow\Cone(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}(S)\rightarrow S)
through
R=A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)/I
, as desired. Thus, (
2.2.1
) is proved.

To perform the missing deformation-theoretic argument, first observe that the image of
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}(S)\rightarrow S
is locally nilpotent (indeed, if
n
is large enough so that
p^{n}=0
in
S
, then
x^{p^{n}}=(p^{n})!\cdot\frac{x^{p^{n}}}{(p^{n})!}=0
for any
x\in S
admitting divided powers), hence
S\rightarrow\Cone(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}(S)\rightarrow S)
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
with locally nilpotent kernel. As the cotangent complex
L\Omega_{R^{\flat}/\mathbb{F}_{p}}
vanishes by virtue of
R^{\flat}
being perfect (see e.g.
[
5
, Lem. 3.5]
), derived deformation theory
1
1

1

More precisely, we are using the following assertion: Let
A
be an animated ring and
B
an
A
-algebra such that
L\Omega_{B/A}
vanishes. For any map of animated
A
-algebras
C^{\prime}\rightarrow C
which is surjective on
\pi_{0}
with locally nilpotent kernel, any
A
-algebra map
B\rightarrow C
lifts uniquely to an
A
-algebra map
B\rightarrow C^{\prime}
. This roughly follows by combining a variant of the proof of
[
33
, Prop. 11.2.1.2]
with a noetherian approximation argument as in the proof of
[
16
, Cor. 5.2.10]
.
shows that the composition

R^{\flat}=\lim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}R/p\rightarrow R/p\rightarrow\Cone(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}(S)\rightarrow S)/{L}p\;,

where the first map is projection onto the first coordinate, lifts uniquely to a map
R^{\flat}\rightarrow S/{L}p
and composition with projection onto
\pi_{0}
yields a map
R^{\flat}\rightarrow S/p
(conversely,
R^{\flat}\rightarrow S/{L}p
can uniquely be recovered from this map as
S/{L}p\rightarrow S/p
is an isomorphism on
\pi_{0}
). Since also
L\Omega_{W(R^{\flat})/p^{n}/\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}
vanishes for any
n\geq 1
(indeed, this may be checked after reduction mod
p

by derived Nakayama
, but
L\Omega_{W(R^{\flat})/p^{n}/\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}\otimes{L}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}\mathbb{F}_{p}\cong L\Omega_{R^{\flat}/\mathbb{F}_{p}}=0
), the same argument shows that this map lifts uniquely to maps
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)=W(R^{\flat})\rightarrow S/p^{n}
. As
S
is
p
-nilpotent, taking
n
sufficiently large, we obtain the desired map
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)\rightarrow S/p^{n}=S
.
∎

The computation above made use of the following statement:

Lemma 2.2.3
.

There is a basis of the fpqc topology consisting of
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}
-acyclic rings (i.e. rings for which all
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}
-torsors are trivial).

Proof.

As
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}
is countably presented, this follows from a variant of the proof of
[
16
, Lem. 5.3.1]
, which we briefly
outline here
: Namely, for any ring
S
, fix a set
\mathcal{S}
of representatives
of
isomorphism classes of countably presented faithfully flat
S
-algebras and put

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}S_{1}\coloneqq\bigotimes_{T\in\mathcal{S}}T\coloneqq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{S}\text{ finite}}\bigotimes_{T\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}T\;.}

Iterating this procedure, we obtain a tower of
faithfully flat

S
-algebras
S_{1}\rightarrow S_{2}\rightarrow\dots
and we put
S_{\omega}\coloneqq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{n}S_{n}
. Continuing in this manner, we obtain
S
-algebras
S_{\alpha}
for each countable ordinal
\alpha
by transfinite induction and finally put
S_{\omega_{1}}\coloneqq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\alpha}S_{\alpha}
.

Now observe that the map
S\rightarrow S_{\omega_{1}}
is a flat cover: Indeed, each
S\rightarrow\bigotimes_{T\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}T
for
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{S}
is a flat cover since flat covers are stable under base change and composition; this then implies that
S_{1}
is flat over
S
as flatness is stable under filtered colimits. Moreover, note that any transition map in the colimit defining
S_{1}
is also faithfully flat; in particular, all transition maps are injective. This means that, for any nonzero
S
-module
M
, we have

M\otimes_{S}S_{1}=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{S}\text{ finite}}M\otimes_{S}\bigotimes_{T\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}T\;,

where each term of the colimit is nonzero and each transition map is injective, hence
M\otimes_{S}S_{1}
is nonzero itself and, consequently,
S_{1}
is a faithfully flat
S
-algebra. Using similar arguments, one can now indeed deduce that
S\rightarrow S_{\omega_{1}}
is a flat cover by transfinite induction.

Now note that

the ring
S_{\omega_{1}}
admits no nonsplit countably presented flat cover
S_{\omega_{1}}\rightarrow R
: indeed, as
R
is a countably presented
S_{\omega_{1}}
-algebra, it descends to a countably presented
S_{\alpha}
-algebra
\widetilde{R}
for some countable ordinal
\alpha
and
S_{\alpha}\rightarrow\widetilde{R}
is faithfully flat since this can be checked after base change to
S_{\omega_{1}}
. However, by construction of
S_{\alpha+1}
, this implies that there is a morphism
\widetilde{R}\rightarrow S_{\alpha+1}\rightarrow S_{\omega_{1}}
of
S_{\alpha}
-algebras, which yields the desired splitting of
S_{\omega_{1}}\rightarrow R
. In particular, we conclude that any
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}
-torsor over
S_{\omega_{1}}
is split, i.e. trivial.

∎

In good cases, coherent cohomology on the de Rham stack of
X
actually computes
the
(
p
-completed) de Rham cohomology of
X
:

Theorem 2.2.4
.

Let
X
be a
smooth qcqs

p
-adic formal scheme and write
\pi_{X^{\mathrm{dR}}}:X^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Then
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dR}}(X)\coloneqq\pi_{X^{\mathrm{dR}},*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{dR}}}
identifies with
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X)
.

Proof.

This is easily deduced from
[
6
, Thm. 2.5.6]
.
∎

Motivated by this result, we make the following definition:

Definition 2.2.5
.

Let
X
be a
smooth qcqs

p
-adic formal scheme. For a quasi-coherent complex
E\in\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{dR}})
, we define the
de Rham cohomology
of
X
with coefficients in
E
as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X,E)\coloneqq R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR}},E)=\pi_{X^{\mathrm{dR}},*}(E)\;.}

Remark 2.2.6
.

In fact, the above notion of coefficients for de Rham cohomology recovers a more classical such notion: Namely, vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
are the same as vector bundles on
X
equipped with a flat connection
having locally nilpotent
p
-curvature after reduction mod
p
(see
[
28
, §5]
for a definition of the notion of
p
-curvature)
– this can be proved using
[
6
, Rem. 2.5.7]
and a similar calculation as in
[
27
, Lem. 3.4.2]
.

To additionally incorporate the Hodge-filtration, we have to consider the following deformation of the stack
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
:

Definition 2.2.7
.

Over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, the canonical map
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}
defines a 1-truncated animated
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-algebra stack

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR},+}\coloneqq\Cone(\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{can}}\mathbb{G}_{a})}

over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
. The
Hodge-filtered de Rham stack

X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
of
X
is the stack
\pi_{X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}:X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
defined by

X^{\mathrm{dR},+}(\Spec S\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\coloneqq\Map(\Spec\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR},+}(S),X)\;,

where the mapping space is computed in derived algebraic geometry.

Remark 2.2.8
.

Observe that the preimage of
\mathbb{G}_{m}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\subseteq\mathbb{A}_{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
under
\pi_{X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
recovers the de Rham stack
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
. The preimage of
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is called the
Hodge stack
of
X
and denoted
X^{\mathrm{Hod}}
.

Example 2.2.9
.

Generalising
Example

2.2.2
, we claim that, for a perfectoid ring
R
, there is an isomorphism of stacks

(2.2.2)

R^{\mathrm{dR},+}\cong\Spf(A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)[\tfrac{u^{n}}{n!},t:n\geq 1]_{(p)}^{\wedge}/(ut-\xi))/\mathbb{G}_{m}

over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
,
where
A=A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)[\tfrac{u^{n}}{n!},t:n\geq 1]_{(p)}^{\wedge}/(ut-\xi)
is equipped with the
(p,\xi)
-adic topology (note that this agrees with the
p
-adic topology due to the existence of divided powers for
u
and hence also for
\xi=ut
) and, as usual,
t
has degree
-1
while
u
has degree
1
. We note here that while
A
is clearly derived
p
-complete, it is also classically
p
-complete since it is
p
-adically separated.

To prove (
2.2.2
), as before, it suffices to describe this isomorphism on
S
-valued points for
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}
-acyclic
p
-nilpotent rings
S
equipped with a morphism
\Spec S\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
by
Lemma

2.2.3
. Then
R^{\mathrm{dR},+}(S)
is the groupoid of maps of animated rings
R\rightarrow\Cone(\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}(S)\rightarrow S)
by
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}
-acyclicity of
S
(any
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}
-torsor
over
S
is also a
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}
-torsor) and if the morphism
\Spec S\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is classified by the generalised Cartier divisor
t:L\rightarrow S
, then
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}(S)
identifies with the set of global sections of
L
equipped with a system of divided powers. Now given any such map
R\rightarrow\Cone(\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}(S)\rightarrow S)
, we obtain a unique lift

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R=A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)/(\xi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cone(\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}(S)\rightarrow S)\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

as in
Example

2.2.2
and then the commutativity of the diagram provides a unique factorisation
S\xrightarrow{u}L\xrightarrow{t}S
of the multiplication-by-
\xi
-map together with a system of divided powers for
u
(i.e. maps
\frac{u^{n}}{n!}:S\rightarrow L^{\otimes n}
).
Together with the map
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)\rightarrow S
we obtained,
this is precisely the datum of an
S
-valued point of
\Spf A/\mathbb{G}_{m}
and the construction is clearly reversible, so we are done.
∎

As expected, coherent cohomology on the Hodge-filtered de Rham stack of
X
computes
the
Hodge-filtered de Rham cohomology of
X
in good cases:

Theorem 2.2.10
.

Let
X
be a
smooth qcqs

p
-adic formal scheme and consider its Hodge-filtered de Rham stack
\pi_{X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}:X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Then
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dR},+}(X)\coloneqq\pi_{X^{\mathrm{dR},+},*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
identifies with
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Hod}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X)
in
\widehat{\mathcal{DF}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
under the Rees equivalence.

Proof.

This is
[
6
, Thm. 2.5.6]
.
∎

Note that the above statement also implies that the pushforward of
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{Hod}}}
to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Hod}}=B\mathbb{G}_{m}
identifies as a graded object with
the
Hodge cohomology of
X
. Again, we are led to the following definition:

Definition 2.2.11
.

Let
X
be a
smooth qcqs

p
-adic formal scheme. For a quasi-coherent complex
E\in\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})
, we define the
Hodge-filtered de Rham cohomology
of
X
with coefficients in
E
as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Hod}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X,E)\coloneqq\pi_{X^{\mathrm{dR},+},*}(E)\;.}

Remark 2.2.12
.

As in the case of the de Rham stack, one can show that the above notion of coefficients for Hodge-filtered de Rham cohomology agrees with a more classical one, see
[
6
, Rem. 2.5.8]
: Namely, a vector bundle on
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is the same as a vector bundle
E
on
X
equipped with a decreasing filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}E
by subbundles and a flat connection
\nabla:E\rightarrow E\otimes\Omega_{X/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{1}
which has nilpotent
p
-curvature mod
p
and satisfies Griffiths transversality with respect to the filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}E
, i.e. we have
\nabla(\Fil^{i}E)\subseteq\Fil^{i-1}E\otimes\Omega_{X/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{1}
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

2.3.
Filtered prismatisation

Having seen stacky formulations of de Rham cohomology and the Hodge filtration, we now describe a similar story for prismatic cohomology and the Nygaard filtration. For the Nygaard-filtered prismatisation, we have chosen to make use of the alternative definition recently given by Gardner–Madapusi in
[
25
, §6.2]
as this will significantly simplify some of the arguments in the upcoming sections.

Definition 2.3.1
.

For a
p
-nilpotent ring
S
, a
Cartier–Witt divisor
on
S
is a generalised Cartier divisor
\alpha:I\rightarrow W(S)
on
W(S)
satisfying the following two conditions:

(i)

The
ideal generated by the
image of the map
I\xrightarrow{\alpha}W(S)\rightarrow S

is nilpotent
.

(ii)

The image of the map
I\xrightarrow{\alpha}W(S)\xrightarrow{\delta}W(S)
generates the unit ideal.

Here,
\delta:W(S)\rightarrow W(S)
is the usual
\delta
-structure on
W(S)
.

Definition 2.3.2
.

The
prismatisation

X{\Delta}
is the stack over
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
given by assigning to a
p
-nilpotent ring
S
the groupoid of pairs

(I\xrightarrow{\alpha}W(S),\Spec\cofib(I\xrightarrow{\alpha}W(S))\rightarrow X)\;,

where the first entry is a Cartier–Witt divisor on
S
and the second entry is a morphism of derived formal schemes.

Example 2.3.3
.

For any Cartier–Witt divisor
I\xrightarrow{\alpha}W(S)
, its Frobenius pullback
F^{*}I\xrightarrow{F^{*}\alpha}W(S)
is again a Cartier–Witt divisor, see
[
6
, Rem. 5.1.10]
, and this induces an endomorphism
F_{X}:X{\Delta}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
called the
Frobenius
on
X{\Delta}
. For
X=\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we just write
F
in place of
F_{X}
; in that case, using the presentation of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}
given in
[
7
, Prop. 3.2.3]
and the fact that the Witt vector Frobenius is faithfully flat, see
[
7
, Prop. 3.4.7]
, one can show that
F:\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}
is an fpqc cover.
∎

Example 2.3.4
.

If
I\xrightarrow{\alpha}W(S)
is a Cartier–Witt divisor, then
I\otimes_{W(S)}S\rightarrow S
is a generalised Cartier divisor on
S
whose image lands inside the nilradical of
S
. Thus, we obtain a map
\mu_{X}:X{\Delta}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
; if
X=\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we just write
\mu
in place of
\mu_{X}
.
∎

Example 2.3.5
.

By
[
6
, Thm. 5.5.7]
, the prismatisation of a quasiregular semiperfectoid ring
R
is given by
R{\Delta}\cong\Spf{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
, where
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R},I)
is the initial object of the absolute prismatic site of
R
and
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
is equipped with the
(p,I)
-adic topology. In particular, if
R
is perfectoid, we have
R{\Delta}\cong\Spf A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)
.
∎

As before, in good situations, coherent cohomology of the structure sheaf on
X{\Delta}
agrees with
the
prismatic cohomology of
X
computed via the absolute prismatic site:

Theorem 2.3.6
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme and
furthermore
assume that
X
is
p
-quasisyntomic and qcqs. Then there is a natural isomorphism

R\Gamma(X{\Delta},\mathcal{O}_{X{\Delta}})\cong R\Gamma{\Delta}(X)\;.

Proof.

Combine
[
8
, Cor. 8.17]
with
[
7
, Thm. 4.4.30]
.
∎

Example 2.3.7
.

There is a certain line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}\{1\}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta})
called the
Breuil–Kisin twist
which will play a key role in the sequel and whose pullback to
X{\Delta}
we will denote by
\mathcal{O}_{X{\Delta}}\{1\}
. Roughly, one can obtain
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}\{1\}
as follows: The category
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta})
identifies with the category of prismatic crystals by
[
7
, Prop. 3.3.5]
and, under this equivalence, the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}\{1\}
corresponds to the prismatic crystal assigning to every prism
(A,I)
the Breuil–Kisin twist
A\{1\}
from
[
7
, Def. 2.5.2]
, which can be thought of heuristically as the infinite tensor product
\bigotimes_{n\geq 0}(\varphi^{n})^{*}I
, where
\varphi
is the Frobenius lift associated to the prism
(A,I)
. Most importantly for what follows, we have
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}\{1\}\otimes F^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}\{-1\}\cong\mathcal{I}
, where
\mathcal{I}\coloneqq\mu^{*}\mathcal{O}(-1)
, see
Example

2.3.4
, and, in particular,

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}\{p-1\}/p\cong\mathcal{I}^{-1}/p\;.\qed

As before, this leads to a notion of prismatic cohomology with coefficients:

Definition 2.3.8
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme which is
p
-quasisyntomic and qcqs. For a quasi-coherent complex
E\in\mathcal{D}(X{\Delta})
, we define the
prismatic cohomology
of
X
with coefficients in
E
as

R\Gamma{\Delta}(X,E)\coloneqq R\Gamma(X{\Delta},E)\;.

We now turn to defining the filtered refinement of
X{\Delta}
computing the Nygaard filtration. For this, let
W
be the ring scheme of
p
-typical Witt vectors, i.e.
W\cong\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Spec\mathbb{Z}[t_{1},t_{2},\dots]
as schemes
so that the functor of points of
W
is given by
S\mapsto W(S)
for any
p
-nilpotent ring
S
via the Witt components
. As usual, we denote the Frobenius and the Verschiebung by
F
and
V
, respectively. Recall from
[
6
, Cor. 2.6.8]
that there is an isomorphism
F_{*}W/{L}p\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}
of
W
-module schemes. Thus, we obtain a map

\widetilde{\mu}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}

by sending a Cartier–Witt divisor
I\xrightarrow{\alpha}W(S)
to the generalised Cartier divisor
F_{*}I\otimes_{F_{*}W(S)}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
on the animated ring
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
.

Definition 2.3.9
.

The stack
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is defined by the pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}
mult

where the bottom map is given by sending a pair
(L\rightarrow S,\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)\rightarrow L^{\prime})
to the generalised Cartier divisor
L\otimes_{S}{L^{\prime}}^{-1}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
on
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
.
The map
t:\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is called the
Rees map
while
\pi:\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}
is called the
structure map
.

Example 2.3.10
.

The inclusion
\mathbb{G}_{m}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
induces an open immersion

j_{\mathrm{dR}}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\;.

Moreover, by the pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}

which in turn comes from the pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{*}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

(note that both rows have fibre
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}
by
[
6
, Rem. 2.6.2]
), the inclusion
(\mathbb{G}_{m}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}\hookrightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}
induces an open immersion

j_{\mathrm{HT}}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}

whose composition with the structure map identifies with the Frobenius endomorphism
F:\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}
defined in
[
7
, Constr. 3.6.1]
.
∎

Example 2.3.11
.

The two maps

\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}\;,\hskip 8.5359pt(L\rightarrow S)\mapsto(L\rightarrow S,\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)\xrightarrow{0}L\otimes_{S}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S))

and
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}
, where the latter map sends any
p
-nilpotent ring
S
to the Cartier–Witt divisor
W(S)\xrightarrow{p}W(S)
, induce a map

i_{\mathrm{dR},+}:\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}

called the
Hodge-filtered de Rham map
. Restricting
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}
to
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}=\mathbb{G}_{m}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
yields maps
i_{\mathrm{dR}}:\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
and
i_{\mathrm{Hod}}:B\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
called the
de Rham map
and the
Hodge map
, respectively. We warn the reader that, despite the notation, the maps
i_{\mathrm{dR},+},i_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
i_{\mathrm{Hod}}
are
not
closed immersions.

∎

To construct
X^{\mathrm{N}}
in general, we use the same procedure as in the case of de Rham cohomology and first construct
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{N}}
. For this, consider an
S
-point of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
; this is the data of (dual) generalised Cartier divisors
L\rightarrow S,\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)\rightarrow L^{\prime}
and a Cartier–Witt divisor
I\rightarrow W(S)
together with an isomorphism

(F_{*}I\otimes_{F_{*}W(S)}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S))\cong(L\otimes_{S}{L^{\prime}}^{-1}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S))

of generalised Cartier divisors on
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
. In particular, we obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{*}I\otimes_{F_{*}W(S)}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\otimes_{S}{L^{\prime}}^{-1}\otimes_{S}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\otimes_{S}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

where the bottom left arrow is induced by
{L^{\prime}}^{-1}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
, and taking horizontal cofibres yields a map

F_{*}W(S)/{L}F_{*}I\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)/{L}(F_{*}I\otimes_{F_{*}W(S)}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S))\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)/{L}(L\otimes_{S}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S))\;.

Definition 2.3.12
.

Let
(L\rightarrow S,\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)\rightarrow L^{\prime},I\rightarrow W(S))
be an
S
-valued point of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
as above. The ring stack
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is defined by the pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{N}}(\Spec S\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{*}W(S)/{L}F_{*}I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S/{L}L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S)/{L}(L\otimes_{S}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}(S))\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

The
Nygaard-filtered prismatisation

X^{\mathrm{N}}
is the stack over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
given by

X^{\mathrm{N}}(\Spec S\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}})\coloneqq\Map(\Spec\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{N}}(\Spec S\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}),X)\;,

where the mapping space is computed in derived algebraic geometry.

All the maps that we have defined above induce corresponding maps for
X^{\mathrm{N}}
in place of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
etc. The situation is summarised by the following commutative diagram:

(2.3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{HT}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{dR},+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{dR}}}

Example 2.3.13
.

By
[
6
, Cor. 5.5.11]
, the Nygaard-filtered prismatisation of a quasiregular semiperfectoid ring
R
is given by

R^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\Spf(\Rees(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{N}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}))/\mathbb{G}_{m}\;,

where
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R},I)
is the initial prism of the absolute prismatic site of
R
as above. In particular, if
R
is perfectoid, using the notation from
Example

2.2.2
, we have

R^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\Spf(A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)\langle u,t\rangle/(ut-\varphi^{-1}(\xi)))/\mathbb{G}_{m}\;,

where
\varphi
is the Frobenius on
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)
and
u
and
t
have degree
1
and
-1
, respectively; we stress that the computation from
[
6
, Ex. 5.5.6]
shows that the notations
u
and
t
for the variables here is compatible with the notation
(t,u)
for the map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}
.
∎

Remark 2.3.14
.

There is an fpqc cover
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle u,t\rangle\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
and hence the stack
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is noetherian and regular. This can be proved by reduction to the perfectoid case treated in the previous example, see
[
6
, Ex. 5.5.20]
for details.

As one should have expected by now, in good situations, coherent cohomology on the
Nygaard-filtered prismatisation
of
X
computes
the
Nygaard-filtered prismatic cohomology of
X
(here, we use the definition of the Nygaard filtration on absolute prismatic cohomology from
[
7
, Def. 5.5.3]
):

Theorem 2.3.15
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme. Assume that
X
is
p
-quasisyntomic and qcqs. Then
t_{X,*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{N}}}
identifies with
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{N}}R\Gamma{\Delta}(X)
in
\widehat{\mathcal{DF}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
under the Rees equivalence.

Proof.

This follows from
[
6
, Cor. 5.5.11, Rem. 5.5.18]
and
[
7
, Cor. 5.5.21]
via quasisyntomic descent.
∎

Example 2.3.16
.

The line bundle

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}}\{1\}\coloneqq\pi^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}\{1\}\otimes t^{*}\mathcal{O}(1)\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}})}

is called the
Breuil–Kisin twist
; via pullback to
X^{\mathrm{N}}
, we obtain line bundles
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{N}}}\{1\}\in\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{N}})
.
∎

Definition 2.3.17
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme which is
p
-quasisyntomic and qcqs. For any
E\in\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{N}})
, we define the
Nygaard-filtered prismatic cohomology
of
X
with coefficients in
E
as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{N}}R\Gamma{\Delta}(X,E)\coloneqq t_{X,*}(E)\;.}

Recalling that the stack
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is noetherian by virtue of the cover
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle u,t\rangle\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
from
Remark

2.3.14
, we can also introduce a reasonable notion of coherent sheaves on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
:

Definition 2.3.18
.

A quasi-coherent complex on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is called
coherent
if its pullback to
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle u,t\rangle
along the map from
Remark

2.3.14
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle u,t\rangle
-module. The full subcategory of
\Perf(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}})
spanned by coherent sheaves is denoted
\Coh(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}})
.

Remark 2.3.19
.

Since
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle u,t\rangle
is regular, one may alternatively describe
\Coh(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}})
as the heart of a canonical
t
-structure on
\Perf(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}})
induced by the
t
-structure on
\Perf(\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle u,t\rangle)
, see
[
6
, Rem. 5.5.19]
.

We conclude this section by giving an alternative description of the Nygaard-filtered prismatisation via descent which is sometimes helpful. For this, recall from
Example

2.3.13
that we can explicitly describe the Nygaard-filtered prismatisation of a quasiregular semiperfectoid ring. Subsequently, one can use the fact that quasiregular semiperfectoid rings form a basis of the quasisyntomic site introduced by Bhatt–Morrow–Scholze in
[
12
, Def. 4.10]
to obtain a description of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
for any
p
-adic formal scheme
X
which is
p
-quasisyntomic and qcqs. For this, one needs to use the following property of the Nygaard-filtered prismatisation:

Lemma 2.3.20
.

Assume that
f:X\rightarrow Y
is a quasi-syntomic cover of
p
-adic formal schemes which are
p
-quasisyntomic and qcqs. Then the induced map
X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
is a flat cover.

Proof.

See
[
25
, Cor. 6.12.8]
.
∎

2.4.
Syntomification

Definition 2.4.1
.

The
syntomification

X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
of
X
is defined as the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}\sqcup X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\;.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{HT}}\sqcup j_{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{N}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j{\Delta}}

Definition 2.4.2
.

The category
\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
is called the category of
F
-gauges
on
X
and denoted
\FGauge{\Delta}(X)
.

Observe that there is an equaliser diagram

(2.4.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{N}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(X{\Delta})\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{N}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{HT}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{*}}

In particular, for any
E\in\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
, there is a fibre sequence

(2.4.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{N}},j_{\mathrm{N}}^{*}E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X{\Delta},j{\Delta}^{*}E)\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{HT}}^{*}-j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{*}}

Example 2.4.3
.

One can check that the Breuil–Kisin twist
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{N}}}\{1\}
descends to a line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}}}\{1\}
. We also call
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}}}\{1\}
the
Breuil–Kisin twist
and denote its
n
-th tensor power by
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}}}\{n\}
.
∎

Example 2.4.4
.

Let
R
be a perfectoid ring. Due to the isomorphism

R^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\Spf(A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)\langle u,t\rangle/(ut-\varphi^{-1}(\xi)))/\mathbb{G}_{m}

from
Example

2.3.13
, a sheaf
E
on
R^{\mathrm{N}}
carries the same data as a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{i+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{i-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

of
(p,\xi)
-complete
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)
-complexes such that
ut=tu=\varphi^{-1}(\xi)
. Writing
M^{-\infty}=(\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i}M^{-i})_{(p,\xi)}^{\wedge}
and
M^{\infty}=(\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i}M^{i})_{(p,\xi)}^{\wedge}
for the
(p,\xi)
-completed colimits along the
t
- and
u
-maps, respectively, the pullback
j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{*}E
identifies with
M^{-\infty}
while
j_{\mathrm{HT}}^{*}E
identifies with
\varphi^{*}M^{\infty}
. Thus, specifying a descent datum of
E
to
R^{\mathrm{Syn}}
amounts to specifying an isomorphism
\tau:\varphi^{*}M^{\infty}\cong M^{-\infty}
. In this situation, observing that
R\Gamma(R^{\mathrm{N}},j_{\mathrm{N}}^{*}E)=M^{0}
, we see that the fibre sequence (
2.4.2
) yields

R\Gamma(R^{\mathrm{Syn}},E)=\fib(M^{0}\xrightarrow{t^{\infty}-\tau u^{\infty}}M^{-\infty})\;.

Here,
t^{\infty}:M^{0}\rightarrow M^{-\infty}
and
u^{\infty}:M^{0}\rightarrow M^{\infty}
denote the maps induced by the
t
- and
u
-maps, respectively, and to define the composition
\tau u^{\infty}
, we use the identification
M^{\infty}\cong\varphi^{*}M^{\infty}
.
∎

Definition 2.4.5
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme and
E\in\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
an
F
-gauge on
X
. Then the pullback of
E
along

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Hod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\;,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{Hod}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{N}}}

where the first map is induced by the canonical map
\mathbb{G}_{a}\rightarrow B\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{a}=\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{Hod}}
of stacks over
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
,
identifies with a graded quasi-coherent complex
M^{\bullet}
on
X
and the set of integers
i
such that
the
i
-th graded piece
M^{i}
is nonzero is called the set of
Hodge–Tate weights
of
E
.

Example 2.4.6
.

The
F
-gauge
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}}}\{i\}
only has a single Hodge–Tate weight, which equals
-i
.
∎

Similar to what we have seen previously, the syntomification and the category of
F
-gauges provide a sensible notion of syntomic cohomology with coefficients:

Definition 2.4.7
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme which is
p
-quasisyntomic and qcqs. For an
F
-gauge
E
on
X
, we define the
syntomic cohomology
of
X
with coefficients in
E
as

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X,E)\coloneqq R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},E)\;.

If
E=\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}}}\{n\}
is a Breuil-Kisin twist, we also write
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{p}(n))
instead of
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X,E)
and we use
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(n))
to denote
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X,E)[\tfrac{1}{p}]
.

In some sense, the category of
F
-gauges on
X
should capture a universal notion of coefficients for various
p
-adic cohomology theories. Evidence for this is given by the existence of certain
realisation functors
from
F
-gauges to coefficients for various other cohomology theories.

Example 2.4.8
.

By functoriality of the syntomification applied to
X_{p=0}\rightarrow X
, we obtain a pullback functor

T_{\mathrm{crys}}:\FGauge{\Delta}(X)\rightarrow\FGauge{\Delta}(X_{p=0})\;,

which we call the
crystalline realisation
.
∎

Example 2.4.9
.

The Hodge-filtered de Rham map
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}:X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}
composed with the map
j_{\mathrm{N}}:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
induces a pullback functor

T_{\mathrm{dR},+}:\FGauge{\Delta}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\;,

which we call the
Hodge-filtered de Rham realisation
. Pulling back further to
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
, we obtain a functor

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T_{\mathrm{dR}}:\FGauge{\Delta}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{dR}})\;,}

which we call the
de Rham realisation
.
∎

There is also an
étale realisation

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}:\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{lisse}}(X_{\eta},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\;.

Here,
X_{\eta}
denotes the generic fibre of
X
regarded as an adic space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{lisse}}(X_{\eta},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denotes the full subcategory of
\mathcal{D}(X_{\eta,\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
spanned by derived
p
-complete locally bounded objects whose mod
p
reduction has locally constant cohomology sheaves with finitely generated stalks.

Using the arc-topology introduced in
[
9
]
, it suffices to describe
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
in the case where
X=\Spf R
is perfectoid by descent, see
[
6
, Constr. 6.3.2]
. Here, in the notation of
Example

2.4.4
, we see that
u^{\infty}
and
t^{\infty}
induce isomorphisms

M^{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{\varphi^{-1}(\xi)}]\xleftarrow{u^{\infty}}M^{0}[\tfrac{1}{\varphi^{-1}(\xi)}]\xrightarrow{t^{\infty}}M^{-\infty}[\tfrac{1}{\varphi^{-1}(\xi)}]\;.

Hence,
\tau
induces an isomorphism

\varphi^{*}M^{-\infty}[\tfrac{1}{\xi}]\cong\varphi^{*}M^{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{\xi}]\cong M^{-\infty}[\tfrac{1}{\xi}]

endowing
M^{-\infty}
with the structure of a prismatic
F
-crystal on
R
. Thus, we obtain a natural map

(2.4.3)

\Perf(R^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\Perf^{\varphi}(A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)[\tfrac{1}{\xi}]^{\wedge}_{(p)})\;,

where the right-hand side denotes the category of perfect complexes over
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)[\tfrac{1}{\xi}]^{\wedge}_{(p)}
equipped with an automorphism linear over the Frobenius of
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)[\tfrac{1}{\xi}]^{\wedge}_{(p)}
.
By
[
14
, Ex. 3.5]
, the latter category identifies with
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{lisse}}(\Spa(R[\tfrac{1}{p}],R),\mathbb{Z}_{p})
via the construction

(2.4.4)

M\mapsto(M\otimes{L}_{A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)}W(R[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\flat}))^{\varphi=1}\;,

where
\varphi
acts diagonally
and the
\varphi
-invariants are taken in the derived sense, i.e. the right-hand side denotes the fibre of the map

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varphi-\id:M\otimes{L}_{A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)}W(R[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\flat})\rightarrow M\otimes{L}_{A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)}W(R[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\flat})\;.}

Remark 2.4.10
.

It is also possible to describe the étale realisation globally (i.e. without using descent): Namely, pullback along either one of the maps
X{\Delta}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
given by
j_{\mathrm{N}}\circ j_{\mathrm{HT}}
or
j_{\mathrm{N}}\circ j_{\mathrm{dR}}
, respectively, induces a functor

(2.4.5)

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\Perf^{\varphi}(X{\Delta})\;,

where the right-hand side denotes the category of prismatic
F
-crystals in perfect complexes on
X
as defined in
[
14
, Rem. 4.2]
and the Frobenius structure comes from the gluing description of
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
using the fact that
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
and
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
differ by a Frobenius twist, see
[
6
, Rem. 6.3.4]
for details. Then the étale realisation is given by the composition

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\Perf^{\varphi}(X{\Delta})\rightarrow\Perf^{\varphi}(X{\Delta},\mathcal{O}{\Delta}[1/\mathcal{I}{\Delta}]_{(p)}^{\wedge})\cong\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{lisse}}(X_{\eta},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\;;

here, the last equivalence is
[
14
, Cor. 3.7]
.

Example 2.4.11
.

One can show that the étale realisation carries the Breuil–Kisin twists
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}}}\{n\}
to the usual Tate twist
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(n)
, see
[
14
, Ex. 4.9]
.
∎

In fact, in the case
X=\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, it turns out that the étale realisation defines an equivalence between a certain full subcategory of
\Perf(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}})
and the category
\Rep_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
of crystalline
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representations in finite free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-modules,
as we will now recall; here,
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
denotes the absolute Galois group of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Proposition 2.4.12
.

Let
R
be a perfectoid valuation ring. Then the following categories are equivalent:

(i)

The category
\Vect^{\varphi}(A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R))
of prismatic
F
-crystals in vector bundles on
R
.

(ii)

The category
\Coh^{\mathrm{refl}}(R^{\mathrm{Syn}})
of reflexive
F
-gauges on
R
as defined in
[
6
, Def. 6.6.4]

Proof.

See
[
6
, Cor. 6.6.5]
.
∎

Remark 2.4.13
.

The functor
\Vect^{\varphi}(A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R))\rightarrow\Coh^{\mathrm{refl}}(R^{\mathrm{Syn}})
can actually be described as follows: To a prismatic
F
-crystal
M
in vector bundles on
R
with
\tau:\varphi^{*}M[\tfrac{1}{\xi}]\cong M[\tfrac{1}{\xi}]
, we associate the
\varphi^{-1}(\xi)
-adically filtered
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R)
-module
\Fil^{\bullet}M
given by
\Fil^{i}M=\varphi_{*}(\xi^{i}M\cap\varphi^{*}M)
, where the intersection takes place inside
\varphi^{*}M[\tfrac{1}{\xi}]\cong M[\tfrac{1}{\xi}]
via
\tau
; by
Example

2.4.4
, this data will determine an
F
-gauge on
R
.

Theorem 2.4.14
.

There is an equivalence of categories

\Coh^{\mathrm{refl}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}})\cong\Vect^{\varphi}((\mathbb{Z}_{p}){\Delta})\cong\Rep_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})

induced by étale realisation. (Here, an
F
-gauge on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is called
reflexive
if its pullback to
\mathcal{O}_{C}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is reflexive.)

Proof.

See
[
6
, Thm. 6.6.13]
.
∎

Actually, it turns out that this allows one to show that the étale realisation of all coherent sheaves on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is a crystalline
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representations as soon as one inverts
p
and that, moreover, in this setting, cohomology of a coherent sheaf agrees with the crystalline part of the Galois cohomology of its étale realisation:

Proposition 2.4.15
.

For any
M\in\Coh(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}})
, the
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representation
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(M)[\tfrac{1}{p}]
is crystalline and the map

R\Gamma(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}},M)[\tfrac{1}{p}]\rightarrow R\Gamma(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(M)[\tfrac{1}{p}])

induced by étale realisation has the following properties:

(i)

It induces an isomorphism on
H^{0}
.

(ii)

It induces an injective map on
H^{1}
with image the subspace of
H^{1}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(M)[\tfrac{1}{p}])
spanned by crystalline extensions of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(M)[\tfrac{1}{p}]
.

(iii)

All
H^{i}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}},M)[\tfrac{1}{p}]
for
i\geq 2
vanish.

Proof.

See
[
6
, Prop. 6.7.3]
.
∎

We now focus on studying
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
further and will hence omit the subscript
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
from the notation for the Breuil–Kisin twists for the purpose of simplification. Namely, we will describe a special locus
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
called the
reduced locus
and this will require us to construct a certain section
v_{1}\in H^{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}},\mathcal{O}\{p-1\}/p)
. There are various ways to do this, e.g. via descent from the quasiregular semiperfectoid case, using a moduli-theoretic description or via the prismatic logarithm, see
[
6
, Constr. 6.2.1]
,
[
18
, §5.10.8]
and
[
10
, Constr. 2.7]
. Here, however, we outline a construction of
v_{1}
that seems to be entirely new and that is based on Gardner–Madapusi’s definition of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
that we are working with in this paper.

For this, note that, while the sheaf
\mathcal{O}(-1)\in\mathcal{D}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
does not descend to
B\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}
, in characteristic
p
, its
p
-th tensor power
\mathcal{O}(-p)\in\mathcal{D}((B\mathbb{G}_{m})_{p=0})
does descend to
(B\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}})_{p=0}
. This is due to the fact that changing a section
u\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(S)
by a section
(v_{1},v_{2},\dots)\in\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\sharp}(S)
for a characteristic
p
ring
S
does not change the
p
-th power of
u
due to

(uv_{1})^{p}=u^{p}(1+(v_{1}-1))^{p}=u^{p}(1+(v_{1}-1)^{p})=u^{p}\;;

this is because elements admitting divided powers have vanishing
p
-th power in characteristic
p
. By essentially the same argument, one sees that
u^{p}:\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(p)
descends from
(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m})_{p=0}
to
(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}_{p=0}
as well.

Lemma 2.4.16
.

On
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}})_{p=0}
, there is an isomorphism
t^{*}\mathcal{O}(-1)\otimes u^{*}\mathcal{O}(p)\cong\mathcal{O}\{p-1\}/p
.

Proof.

We work over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
throughout and thus mostly suppress the subscript
(-)_{p=0}
from the notation. Recalling that
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}\{p-1\}/p\cong\mathcal{I}^{-1}/p
from
Example

2.3.7
, all we have to show is that

u^{*}\mathcal{O}(p)\cong\pi^{*}\mathcal{I}^{-1}/p\otimes t^{*}\mathcal{O}(p)\;.

To this end, consider the pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

defining
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
and note that
\mathrm{mult}^{*}\mathcal{O}(-p)\cong\mathcal{O}(-p)\boxtimes\mathcal{O}(p)
and that the further pullback of this along
(t,u)
is given by
t^{*}\mathcal{O}(-p)\otimes u^{*}\mathcal{O}(p)
. Thus, considering the pullback of
\mathcal{O}(-p)
from
(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}
to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
along the other path in the diagram, it suffices to prove that the pullback of
\mathcal{O}(-p)
along
\widetilde{\mu}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}
is given by
\mathcal{I}^{-1}/p
.

We first claim that there is a map
\widetilde{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{O}(p)\rightarrow\mathcal{I}/p
. For this, it suffices to show that the closed substack of
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta})_{p=0}
cut out by the ideal sheaf
\mathcal{I}/p
lands inside the closed substack of
(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}
cut out by
t^{p}:\mathcal{O}(p)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}
, which is a well-defined section by the discussion above. To see this, we invoke the fact that the former is the (mod
p
reduction of the) Hodge–Tate stack of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and thus isomorphic to
B\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\sharp}
, the classifying stack of the PD hull of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
at
1
, with the quotient map
\Spec\mathbb{F}_{p}\rightarrow B\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\sharp}\subseteq(\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta})_{p=0}
classifying the Cartier–Witt divisor
p:W(\mathbb{F}_{p})\rightarrow W(\mathbb{F}_{p})
, see
[
7
, §3.4]
. As we may work fpqc locally, all we have to check is that this particular Cartier–Witt divisor lands in the vanishing locus of
t^{p}
under
\widetilde{\mu}
, but this is true by the moduli description of the map
\widetilde{\mu}
.

To check that the map
\widetilde{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{O}(p)\rightarrow\mathcal{I}/p
constructed above is an isomorphism, we may now pull back along the fpqc cover
F:\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}
. However, using the commutative diagram (it is in fact a pullback square)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}

from
Example

2.3.10
, we may compute
F^{*}\widetilde{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{O}(p)
alternatively as the pullback of
\mathcal{O}(p)
along
\mu:\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, which comes out to be
\mathcal{I}^{p}/p
. Moreover, as
F:\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}
lifts the Frobenius on
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta})_{p=0}
by
[
7
, Rem. 3.6.2]
, the pullback of
\mathcal{I}/p
along
F
is
\mathcal{I}^{p}/p
as well and one can check that, under these identifications, the map constructed is just the identity on
\mathcal{I}^{p}/p
.
∎

Definition 2.4.17
.

Under the isomorphism
\mathcal{O}\{p-1\}/p\cong t^{*}\mathcal{O}(-1)\otimes u^{*}\mathcal{O}(p)
from the lemma above, we define the section

v_{1}\in H^{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}},\mathcal{O}\{p-1\}/p)

as the one identifying with the pullback of
u^{p}t
.

Tracing through the identifications, to see that
v_{1}
actually descends to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
, we have to identify the pullback of the section
u^{p}:\mathcal{O}(-p)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}
along
\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}
with the pullback of the section
t:\mathcal{O}(1)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}
along
\mu
. However, this actually already follows from the proof above.

Definition 2.4.18
.

The
reduced locus

\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is the vanishing locus of
(p,v_{1})
. Its pullback to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is called the
reduced locus
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
and denoted
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{N}}
.

It turns out that there is a very concrete way to describe
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and cohomology on it. To explain this, we need to consider several components of
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
. Note that we are now working over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.

(i)

The
conjugate-filtered Hodge–Tate component

\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT},c}^{\mathrm{N}}
is the closed substack of
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}})_{p=0}
cut out by the equation
t=0
and one can show that
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT},c}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a,+}/(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m})
, see
[
6
, Prop. 5.3.7]
. Thus, the stack
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT},c}^{\mathrm{N}}
admits the following stratification:

(1)

The substack
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT}}^{\mathrm{N}}\coloneqq B\Stab_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}}(1\in\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong B\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\sharp}
is open and one can show that it identifies with
j_{\mathrm{HT}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta})\cap\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{N}}
.

(2)

The substack
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{N}}\coloneqq B\Stab_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}}(0\in\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong B(F_{*}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m})
is closed and identifies with the reduced complement of
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT}}^{\mathrm{N}}
.

(ii)

The
Hodge-filtered de Rham component

\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}^{\mathrm{N}}
is the closed substack of
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}})_{p=0}
cut out by the equation
u^{p}=0
. As
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}:\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}^{\mathrm{N}}
is an fpqc cover splitting the restriction
t_{\mathrm{dR},+}:\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
of the Rees map, we have
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong B_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathcal{G}
, where
\mathcal{G}
denotes the group scheme
\Aut(i_{\mathrm{dR},+})
. One can explicitly compute
\mathcal{G}
, see
[
18
, Ch. 7]
; for us, however, it is only important that
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}^{\mathrm{N}}
admits the following stratification:

(1)

The preimage of
\Spec\mathbb{F}_{p}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
under
t_{\mathrm{dR},+}
identifies with the open substack
j_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta})\cap\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{N}}
and
is
denoted
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{N}}
. Up to a Frobenius twist, which is trivial here, it is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT}}^{\mathrm{N}}
.

(2)

The preimage of
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
under
t_{\mathrm{dR},+}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{N}}
.

To obtain
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{N}}
from these components, one glues
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT},c}^{\mathrm{N}}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}^{\mathrm{N}}
along
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{N}}
. Further gluing
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT}}^{\mathrm{N}}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{N}}
along a Frobenius twist, which is trivial in our situation, then yields
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
. Thus, to study cohomology on
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
, we need to study cohomology on the loci described above, starting with
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{N}}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{N}}
:

(1)

For
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong B\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\sharp}
, one can show that

\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{N}})\cong\mathcal{D}_{(\Theta^{p}-\Theta)-\mathrm{nilp}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}[\Theta])\;,

where the latter category consists of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-complexes
V
equipped an operator
\Theta
such that the action of
\Theta^{p}-\Theta
on cohomology is locally nilpotent, see
[
7
, Thm. 3.5.8]
. Under this equivalence, the Breuil–Kisin twist
\mathcal{O}\{n\}
corresponds to
(\mathbb{F}_{p},\Theta=n)
and if
E\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{N}})
corresponds to
(V,\Theta)
, then

R\Gamma(\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{N}},E)=\fib(V\xrightarrow{\Theta}V)\;,

see
[
7
, Ex. 3.5.6, Prop. 3.5.11]
.

(2)

For
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong B(F_{*}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m})
, one can show that

\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{N}})\cong\mathcal{D}_{\gr,\Theta-\mathrm{nilp}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}[\Theta])\;,

where
\Theta
has degree
-p
, i.e. the latter category consists of
\mathbb{Z}
-indexed collections of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-complexes
V_{i}
equipped with operators
\Theta_{i}:V_{i}\rightarrow V_{i-p}
such that the action of
\Theta=\bigoplus_{i}\Theta_{i}
on cohomology is locally nilpotent. Under this equivalence, the Breuil–Kisin twist
\mathcal{O}\{n\}
corresponds to the vector space
\mathbb{F}_{p}
sitting in grading degree
-n
and if
E\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{N}})
corresponds to
(\{V_{i}\},\{\Theta_{i}\})
, then

R\Gamma(\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{N}},E)=\fib(V_{0}\xrightarrow{\Theta}V_{-p})\;.

On
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}^{\mathrm{N}}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT},c}^{\mathrm{N}}
, we obtain the following descriptions of quasi-coherent complexes:

(i)

For
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT},c}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}/(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\sharp}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m})
, one can show that

\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT},c}^{\mathrm{N}})\cong\mathcal{D}_{\gr,D-\mathrm{nilp}}(\mathcal{A}_{1})\;,

where
\mathcal{A}_{1}\coloneqq\mathbb{F}_{p}\{x,D\}/(Dx-xD-1)
with
D
having degree
-1
and
x
having degree
1
; here, the notation
\mathbb{F}_{p}\{x,D\}
refers to the free (non-commutative!)
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra on the variables
x
and
D
. Alternatively, via the Rees equivalence, we may describe any
E\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT},c}^{\mathrm{N}})
as an increasingly filtered
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-complex
\Fil_{\bullet}V
together with operators
D:\Fil_{\bullet}V\rightarrow\Fil_{\bullet-1}V
which are locally nilpotent in cohomology such that
Dx=xD+1
, where
x:\Fil_{\bullet}V\rightarrow\Fil_{\bullet+1}V
denotes the transition maps.
