\pi_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}},*}E
has Hodge–Tate weights all at most
-p-1
. Thus, we conclude

F_{\mathrm{Hod}}(E)=\fib(\gr^{0}V\xrightarrow{\Theta}\gr^{-p}V)=0\;.

Moreover, as the filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}V
is again
complete by
Lemma

B.0.3
, we also have
\Fil^{0}V=\Fil^{-p}V=0
and hence also

F_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E)=\fib(\Fil^{0}V\xrightarrow{\Theta}\Fil^{-p}V)=0\;.

Finally, we see that
\Fil_{-1}V=\Fil_{0}V=V
and thus

F_{\mathrm{HT},c}(E)=\fib(\Fil_{-1}V\xrightarrow{D}\Fil_{0}V)=\fib(V\xrightarrow{\Theta}V)=F_{\mathrm{dR}}(E)\;.

Overall, using (
2.4.6
), we obtain

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}}_{\mathrm{red}},E)=\fib(F_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E)\oplus F_{\mathrm{HT},c}(E)\xrightarrow{a_{E}-b_{E}}F_{\mathrm{dR}}(E)\oplus F_{\mathrm{Hod}}(E))=0\;,

as wanted.
∎

5.
A comparison of crystalline cohomology and étale cohomology

In this final section, we establish a generalisation of a comparison theorem of Colmez–Nizioł between rational crystalline cohomology and étale cohomology, see
[
15
, Cor. 1.4]
, which allows for coefficients.

Theorem 5.0.1
.

Let
X
be a
p
-adic formal scheme
which is smooth and proper over
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. For any crystalline local system
L
on
X_{\eta}
with Hodge–Tate weights all at most
-i-1
for some
i\geq 0
, let
\mathcal{E}
be its associated
F
-isocrystal. Then there is a natural morphism

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{crys}}(X_{p=0},\mathcal{E})[\tfrac{1}{p}][-1]\rightarrow R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},L)[\tfrac{1}{p}]\;,

which induces an isomorphism

\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{crys}}(X_{p=0},\mathcal{E})[\tfrac{1}{p}][-1]\cong\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},L)[\tfrac{1}{p}]

and an injection on
H^{i+1}
.

In order to clear up all the terms appearing in the statement, we will first recall the basic definitions surrounding crystalline local systems. Afterwards, we will go on to proving
Theorem

5.0.1
, for which we will roughly proceed as follows: First, we show how to associate to any crystalline local system
L
on
X_{\eta}
an
F
-gauge
E
on
X
with the property that
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(E)\cong L
and
T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E)\cong\mathcal{E}
under the identification
X^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong(X_{p=0}){\Delta}
from
[
6
, Constr. 3.1.1]
. Then we prove that

\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X,T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E))[\tfrac{1}{p}][-1]\cong\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(E))[\tfrac{1}{p}]

for this
F
-gauge
E
using the results from Section
3
and Section
4
.

5.1.
Recollections on crystalline local systems

We briefly recap the theory of crystalline local systems along the lines of
[
26
, Sect. 2]
and start by recalling the definition of an
F
-isocrystal. For this, let
Y
be an
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-scheme.

Definition 5.1.1
.

Consider the standard divided power structure on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(i.e. the one given by
\gamma_{n}(p)=\tfrac{p^{n}}{n!}
for
n\geq 0
). A
crystal
(in coherent sheaves) on
Y
is a sheaf
\mathcal{E}
of
\mathcal{O}_{Y/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-modules on the big crystalline site
(Y/\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathrm{crys}}
of
Y
satisfying the following two properties:

(i)

For each object
(U,T,\gamma)
of
(Y/\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathrm{crys}}
, the restriction
\mathcal{E}|_{T}
of
\mathcal{E}
to the Zariski-site of
T
is a coherent sheaf.

(ii)

For each map
\alpha:(U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})\rightarrow(U,T,\gamma)
in
(Y/\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathrm{crys}}
, the induced map
\alpha^{*}(\mathcal{E}|_{T^{\prime}})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}|_{T}
is an
\mathcal{O}_{T}
-linear isomorphism.

We denote the category of crystals on
Y
by
\Crys(Y/\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and call the isogeny category

\Isoc(Y/\mathbb{Z}_{p})\coloneqq\Crys(Y/\mathbb{Z}_{p})[\tfrac{1}{p}]

the category of
isocrystals
on
Y
.

The absolute Frobenius on
Y
and the Witt vector Frobenius on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(which is the identity in our case) are compatible and hence we obtain a morphism of sites

F:(Y/\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathrm{crys}}\rightarrow(Y/\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathrm{crys}}

inducing a morphism of the corresponding topoi.

Definition 5.1.2
.

Let
Y
be an
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-scheme. An
F
-isocrystal
on
Y
is an isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
on
Y
equipped with an isomorphism
\varphi:F^{*}\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
. We use
\Isoc^{\varphi}(Y/\mathbb{Z}_{p})
to denote the category of
F
-isocrystals on
Y
.

To proceed further, we need to introduce certain period rings on the pro-étale site of any locally noetherian adic space
W
over
\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. As affinoid perfectoid objects of
W_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
form a basis of the topology by
[
37
, Lem. 4.6, Prop. 4.8]
, a sheaf on
W_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
can be defined by giving its values on affinoid perfectoid objects; since
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow C
is pro-étale, we may furthermore restrict to affinoid perfectoids over
\Spa C
, where we recall that
C
denotes a fixed completed algebraic closure of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Definition 5.1.3
.

Let
W
be a locally noetherian adic space over
\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The sheaves
\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}},\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
on
W_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
are defined as follows: For any affinoid perfectoid
\Spa(R,R^{+})\in W_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
over
\Spa C
, we define

(i)

\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(R,R^{+})\coloneqq A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R^{+})
, where we recall that the right-hand side was defined in
Example

2.2.2
,

(ii)

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(R,R^{+})\coloneqq\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(R,R^{+})[\tfrac{1}{p}]
,

(iii)

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(R,R^{+})\coloneqq\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(R,R^{+})[\tfrac{1}{\mu}]
, where
\mu\coloneqq[\varepsilon^{1/p}]-1\in A_{\mathrm{inf}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})
is defined as in the proof of
Lemma

3.3.2
.

Now assume that
W=X_{\eta}
is the generic fibre of a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme
X
. In this case, we can define a variant of the sheaf
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
with coefficients in any
F
-isocrystal on the special fibre
X_{p=0}
:

Definition 5.1.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme and
\mathcal{E}\in\Isoc^{\varphi}(X_{p=0}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})
an
F
-isocrystal on the special fibre of
X
. Then we define sheaves
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(\mathcal{E})
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
on
X_{\eta,\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
as follows: For an affinoid perfectoid
\Spa(R,R^{+})\in X_{\eta,\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
over
\Spa C
, we define

(i)

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(\mathcal{E})(R,R^{+})\coloneqq\mathcal{E}(A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R^{+}))[\tfrac{1}{p}]
, where

\mathcal{E}(A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R^{+}))\coloneqq\lim_{n}\mathcal{E}(A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R^{+})/p,A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R^{+})/p^{n},\gamma)

and
\gamma
denotes the canonical divided power structure,

(ii)

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})(R,R^{+})\coloneqq\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(\mathcal{E})(R,R^{+})[\tfrac{1}{\mu}]
.

Note that the Frobenius on
A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R^{+})
for any affinoid perfectoid
\Spa(R,R^{+})
over
\Spa C
induces a Frobenius
F:\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
on the sheaf
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
and we have an isomorphism
F^{*}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\cong\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(F^{*}\mathcal{E})
for any
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
on
X_{p=0}
. Thus, we obtain a Frobenius

\varphi:F^{*}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\cong\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(F^{*}\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})

on
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
by functoriality of the assignment
\mathcal{E}\mapsto\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
.

By
[
26
, Prop. 2.38]
, we may now define a crystalline local system as follows:

Definition 5.1.5
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme. A
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-local system
L
on
X_{\eta,\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is called
crystalline
if there exists an
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
on
X_{p=0}
and an isomorphism

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\cong\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}L

compatible with the Frobenius on both sides. In this case, we call
\mathcal{E}
the
associated
F
-isocrystal
of
L
. Here,
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
denotes the sheaf
\lim_{n}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
on
X_{\eta,\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, as usual.

Remark 5.1.6
.

Intuitively, one should think of a crystalline local system on
X_{\eta}
as a continuous family of crystalline representations. Indeed, in yet unpublished work, Guo–Yang show that a local system
L
on
X_{\eta}
is crystalline if and only if its restrictions to all closed points of
X_{\eta}
are crystalline representations, see
[
26
, Rem. 2.32]
. In particular, this also implies that the notion of a local system on
X_{\eta}
being crystalline is independent of the choice of the smooth integral model
X
.

Finally, we need to establish the notion of
Hodge–Tate weights
of a crystalline local system
L
on
X_{\eta}
. For this, we first need to define even more period sheaves.

Definition 5.1.7
.

Let
W
be a locally noetherian adic space over
\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The sheaves
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
on
W_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
are defined as follows: For any affinoid perfectoid
\Spa(R,R^{+})\in W_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
over
\Spa C
, we define

(i)

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R,R^{+})\coloneqq A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R^{+})[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\wedge}_{\Ker\theta}
equipped with the
(\Ker\theta)
-adic filtration, where
\theta:A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R^{+})[\tfrac{1}{p}]\rightarrow R
is induced by the usual Fontaine map,

(ii)

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(R,R^{+})\coloneqq\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R,R^{+})[\tfrac{1}{t}]
with the filtration

\Fil^{n}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}\coloneqq\sum_{m\geq-n}t^{-m}\Fil^{m+n}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R,R^{+})\;,

where
t\coloneqq\log[\varepsilon]\in A_{\mathrm{crys}}(R,R^{+})
is defined as in (
3.2.1
).

Definition 5.1.8
.

Let
W
be a locally noetherian adic space over
\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The sheaf
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
on
W_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is defined as follows: For any affinoid perfectoid
\Spa(R,R^{+})=\lim_{i}\Spa(R_{i},R_{i}^{+})\in W_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
over
\Spa C
, we define

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R,R^{+})\coloneqq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i}(R_{i}^{+}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R^{+}))[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\wedge}_{\Ker\theta}\;,}

where we abuse notation and denote by
\theta:(R_{i}^{+}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R^{+}))[\tfrac{1}{p}]\rightarrow R
the map induced by the two maps
R_{i}\rightarrow R
and
A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R^{+})\rightarrow R
. We equip
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R,R^{+})
with the
(\Ker\theta)
-adic filtration and hence obtain a decreasing filtration on
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R,R^{+})[\tfrac{1}{t}]
given by

\Fil^{n}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R,R^{+})[\tfrac{1}{t}]\coloneqq\sum_{m\geq-n}t^{-m}\Fil^{m+n}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R,R^{+})\;.

This defines a filtered sheaf on
W_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
whose completion with respect to the filtration we denote by
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
and call the
structural de Rham sheaf
. Its associated graded is the
structural Hodge–Tate sheaf
denoted by
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{HT}}
.

Now recall that being crystalline implies being de Rham in the sense of
[
37
, Def. 8.3]
by
[
26
, Cor. 2.37]
and thus,
a fortiori
, also being Hodge–Tate in the sense of
[
38
, Def. 5.6]
, see
[
34
, Cor. 3.12]
. In particular, this means that the sheaves

(5.1.1)

D_{\mathrm{dR}}(L)\coloneqq\nu_{*}(L\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}})

and

(5.1.2)

D_{\mathrm{HT}}(L)\coloneqq\nu_{*}(L\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{HT}})

are vector bundles on
X_{\eta,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Here, the map
\nu:X_{\eta,\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow X_{\eta,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
denotes the canonical projection; however, we warn the reader that, contrary to our usual convention that all pushforwards are automatically derived, the pushforward
\nu_{*}
appearing in (
5.1.2
) and (
5.1.1
) is
underived
.

Definition 5.1.9
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme and
L
a crystalline local system on the generic fibre
X_{\eta}
. The
Hodge–Tate weights
of
L
are those integers
i\in\mathbb{Z}
such that the graded vector bundle
D_{\mathrm{HT}}(L)
is non-trivial in grading degree
i
.

Remark 5.1.10
.

Note that, by definition, the sheaf
D_{\mathrm{HT}}(L)
is the associated graded of the filtered vector bundle
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(L)
. Thus, one may alternatively define the Hodge–Tate weights of
L
as the integers
i\in\mathbb{Z}
for which the filtration on
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(L)
jumps.

Remark 5.1.11
.

If
X_{\eta}
is geometrically connected, by
[
38
, Thm. 1.1]
, we may alternatively define the Hodge–Tate weights of
L
as the Hodge–Tate weights of the
p
-adic Galois representation
L_{\overline{x}}
of
k(x)
, where
x
denotes any classical point of
X_{\eta}
(i.e. any point of
X_{\eta}
seen as a rigid-analytic variety) and
k(x)
is its residue field.

5.2.
F
-gauges associated to crystalline local systems

We first show how to associate an
F
-gauge to any crystalline local system on the generic fibre of a smooth formal scheme
X
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. To this end, recall that,
in independent work, both
Guo–Reinecke
and Du–Liu–Moon–Shimizu
have recently given a description of crystalline local systems on
X_{\eta}
in terms of the prismatic site of
X
, which we review here briefly,
see
[
26
]
and
[
17
]
.

Definition 5.2.1
.

For any prism
(A,I)
, let
\Vect^{\varphi}(\Spec(A)\setminus V(p,I))
denote the category of vector bundles
M
on
\Spec(A)\setminus V(p,I)
together with an
A
-linear isomorphism
\varphi^{*}M[1/I]\cong M[1/I]
, where
\varphi
is the Frobenius of
A
. Then the category of
analytic prismatic
F
-crystals
on
X
is defined as

\Vect^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(X{\Delta})\coloneqq\lim_{(A,I)\in X{\Delta}}\Vect^{\varphi}(\Spec(A)\setminus V(p,I))\;.

In our setting, it turns out that crystalline local systems and analytic prismatic
F
-crystals are the same:

Proposition 5.2.2
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme. Then there is a natural equivalence of categories

\Vect^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(X{\Delta})\cong\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})\;.

Proof.

See
[
26
, Thm. A]

or
[
17
, Thm. 1.3, Rem. 1.5]
.

∎

Next, given an analytic prismatic
F
-crystal on
X
, we extend it to a prismatic
F
-crystal in perfect complexes on
X
by (underived) pushforward along the open immersion

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Spec(A)\setminus V(p,I)\rightarrow\Spec A}

for all
(A,I)\in X{\Delta}
.

Proposition 5.2.3
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme. Then
(underived)
pushforward along the open immersions
\Spec(A)\setminus V(p,I)\rightarrow\Spec A
for all
(A,I)\in X{\Delta}
induces a fully faithful
embedding

\Vect^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(X{\Delta})\hookrightarrow\Perf^{\varphi}(X{\Delta})

from the category of analytic prismatic
F
-crystals to the category of prismatic
F
-crystals in perfect complexes.

Proof.

See
[
26
, Thm. 5.10]
.
∎

To finish the construction, we have to show how to extend a prismatic
F
-crystal in perfect complexes
M
, i.e. a perfect complex on
X{\Delta}
together with a Frobenius structure, to a perfect complex on
X^{\mathrm{N}}
. The Frobenius structure will ensure that the result descends to
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
, see
[
6
, Ex. 6.1.7]
. By quasisyntomic descent, see
Lemma

2.3.20
, it suffices to describe the procedure for
X=\Spf R
with a quasiregular semiperfectoid ring
R
. Here, we have

R^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\Spf\Rees(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{N}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})/\mathbb{G}_{m}

by
Example

2.3.13
and thus we need to equip
M
with a decreasing filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}M
compatible with the Nygaard filtration on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
. This construction is supplied by the following definition from
[
6
, Def. 6.6.6]
:

Definition 5.2.4
.

Let
R
be a quasiregular semiperfectoid ring and
(M,\tau)\in\Mod^{\varphi}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})
be a prismatic
F
-crystal in finitely presented modules on
\Spf R
. A
Nygaardian filtration
on
M
is a filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}M
of
M
in
(p,I)
-complete
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
-modules such that the map

M\xrightarrow{\mathrm{can}}\varphi^{*}M\hookrightarrow\varphi^{*}M[1/I]\overset{\tau}{\cong}M[1/I]

carries
\Fil^{i}M
into
I^{i}M
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
; here,
I
is the effective Cartier divisor on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
defining the initial prism of
R{\Delta}
. A Nygaardian filtration is called
saturated
if it is maximal, i.e. if
\Fil^{\bullet}M
is the preimage of
I^{\bullet}M
under the map above.

Indeed, this construction gives what we want provided we impose a small restriction on the class of prismatic
F
-crystals in perfect complexes we are considering.

Definition 5.2.5
.

Let
X
be a
p
-quasisyntomic

p
-adic formal scheme. We define the category
\Perf^{\varphi}_{I-\mathrm{tf}}(X{\Delta})
to be the full subcategory of
\Perf^{\varphi}(X{\Delta})
consisting of prismatic
F
-crystals
\mathcal{E}
satisfying the following property: For any quasiregular semiperfectoid ring
R
such that
\Spf R\rightarrow X
is
p
-quasisyntomic
, the complex
\mathcal{E}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})
is concentrated in cohomological degree zero and has no
I
-torsion, where again
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R},I)
is the inital object of
R{\Delta}
.

Proposition 5.2.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme. Then there is a fully faithful embedding

\Perf^{\varphi}_{I-\mathrm{tf}}(X{\Delta})\hookrightarrow\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})

characterised by the requirement that its composition with pullback along
R^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
for any quasiregular semiperfectoid ring
R
such that
\Spf R\rightarrow X
is
p
-completely flat sends a prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}
to the module
\mathcal{E}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})
equipped with its saturated Nygaardian filtration.

Proof.

See
[
24
, Thm. 2.31]
.
∎

To be able to apply the above proposition in our situation, we only need to check that the essential image of the functor from
Proposition

5.2.3
is contained in
\Perf^{\varphi}_{I-\mathrm{tf}}(X{\Delta})
. However, for any quasiregular semiperfectoid ring
R
, any object in the essential image of

\Vect^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(R{\Delta})\rightarrow\Perf^{\varphi}(R{\Delta})\cong\Perf^{\varphi}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})

identifies with the global sections of a vector bundle on
\Spec({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})\setminus V(p,I)
and this immediately implies the claim as
I
is an effective Cartier divisor on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
and hence
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
has no
I
-torsion.

We summarise the results we have collected so far:

Theorem 5.2.7
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme. Then there is a fully faithful embedding

\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})\hookrightarrow\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})

which is a weak right inverse to the étale realisation. The image of a crystalline local system
L
under this embedding is called the
associated
F
-gauge

E
of
L
and has the property that
T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E)
identifies with the associated
F
-isocrystal of
L
.

Proof.

The existence of the embedding follows by combining
Proposition

5.2.2
,
Proposition

5.2.3
and
Proposition

5.2.6
; moreover,
by the description of the étale realisation from
Remark

2.4.10
,
the relation with the étale realisation is clear from the construction:
indeed, the functor from
Proposition

5.2.6
is a weak right inverse to the functor
\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\Perf^{\varphi}(X{\Delta})
from (
2.4.5
) and this implies the claim.
Finally, the last
statement about compatibility with the de Rham realisation
follows from the commutativity of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{p=0}){\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

and the fact that the associated
F
-isocrystal of
L
can be obtained from the associated analytic prismatic
F
-crystal via
the composite functor

\Vect^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(X{\Delta})\rightarrow\Vect^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}((X_{p=0}){\Delta})\cong\Isoc^{\varphi}(X_{p=0}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})

from

[
26
, Constr. 3.9.(ii)]
, see the proof of
[
26
, Thm. 3.12]
.
∎

Definition 5.2.8
.

The essential image of the embedding

\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})\hookrightarrow\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})

is called the category of
reflexive
F
-gauges
on
X
and denoted by
\Coh^{\mathrm{refl}}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
.

Remark 5.2.9
.

We shortly discuss why the notions of Hodge–Tate weights of a crystalline local system
L
and its associated
F
-gauge
E
are compatible. By
Remark

5.1.10
, this would follow from the fact that
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(L)
can be identified with (the pullback to
X_{\eta}
of)
T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E)
. Using
[
37
, Cor. 6.19]
, it then suffices to prove that there is a filtered isomorphism

L\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}\cong T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}\;.

However, the unfiltered isomorphism exists by
[
26
, Prop. 2.36]
and the fact that
T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E)
identifies with the associated
F
-isocrystal of
L
. To check that this isomorphism respects the filtrations, we may now proceed locally on
X_{\eta,\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
and hence assume
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\cong\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}[[X_{1},\dots,X_{n}]]
by
[
37
, Prop. 6.10]
. Then we are reduced to showing that

L\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}\cong T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}

compatibly with the filtrations on either side, which may be checked on affinoid perfectoid objects
\Spa(R,R^{+})\in X_{\eta,\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
over
\Spa C
such that
L|_{\Spa(R,R^{+})}
is trivial since such objects form a basis of
X_{\eta,\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
– however, in this case, the claim follows by virtually the same proof as
Lemma

3.3.4
with the use of
[
11
, Lem. 4.26]
replaced by
[
26
, Lem. 3.13]
.

5.3.
Proof of the main theorem

We now move on to prove the necessary comparison results for reflexive
F
-gauges. However, for simplicity, we start with the case of vector bundles:

Proposition 5.3.1
.

Let
X
be a
p
-adic formal scheme
which is smooth and proper over
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. For any vector bundle
F
-gauge
E\in\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
with Hodge–Tate weights all at most
-i-1
for some
i\geq 0
, there is a natural morphism

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X,T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E))[\tfrac{1}{p}][-1]\rightarrow R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(E))[\tfrac{1}{p}]\;,

which induces an isomorphism

\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X,T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E))[\tfrac{1}{p}][-1]\cong\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(E))[\tfrac{1}{p}]

and an injection on
H^{i+1}
.

Proof.

Combining
Theorem

3.5.4
and
Proposition

B.0.1
, we obtain a fibre sequence

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X,T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E))[\tfrac{1}{p}][-1]\rightarrow R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X,E)[\tfrac{1}{p}]\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Fil^{0}_{\mathrm{Hod}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X,T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E))[\tfrac{1}{p}]}\;.

In view of
Theorem

4.0.2
, it thus suffices to show that
\Fil^{0}_{\mathrm{Hod}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X,T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E))
is concentrated in degrees at least
i+1
. To this end, identify
T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(\pi_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}},*}E)
with a filtered object
\Fil^{\bullet}V

and let
d
be the relative dimension of
X
over
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Then
Proposition

4.2.11
implies that
\gr^{\bullet}V=0
for
\bullet\geq d-i
and that, moreover,
\gr^{d-i-1-k}V
is concentrated in degrees at least
d-k
for
0\leq k\leq d
. As the filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}V
is
complete by
Lemma

B.0.3
, we may
thus
conclude that
\Fil^{0}V
is concentrated in degrees at least
i+1
, which yields the claim.
∎

We can also formulate an analogous result for arbitrary perfect
F
-gauges, albeit in a slightly weaker range:

Proposition 5.3.2
.

Let
X
be a
p
-adic formal scheme
which is proper and smooth
of relative dimension
d
over
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. For any perfect
F
-gauge
E\in\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
with Hodge–Tate weights all at most
-d-2
for some
i\geq 0
, there is a natural isomorphism

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X,T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E))[\tfrac{1}{p}][-1]\cong R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(E))[\tfrac{1}{p}]\;.

Proof.

As in the proof of
Proposition

5.3.1
, now using
Theorem

4.0.1
in place of
Theorem

4.0.2
, we are reduced to showing that
\Fil^{0}_{\mathrm{Hod}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X,T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E))=0
. However,
by
Lemma

B.0.3
, the filtered object
T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(\pi_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}},*}E)\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
is
complete
and
Proposition

4.2.11
shows that

\pi_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}},*}E
has Hodge–Tate weights all at most
-2
, hence
T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(\pi_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}},*}E)
is zero in filtration degree zero and this implies the claim.
∎

Now observe that, by
Theorem

5.2.7
, having the conclusion of
Proposition

5.3.1
for reflexive
F
-gauges instead of just vector bundle
F
-gauges would immediately imply
Theorem

5.0.1
.
This will be a consequence of the following lemma:

Lemma 5.3.3
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme and
E\in\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
a reflexive
F
-gauge. Then the pullback of
E
to
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{\mathrm{Hod}}
is concentrated in cohomological degree zero.

Proof.

By quasisyntomic descent, see
Lemma

2.3.20
, we may check this in the case where
X=\Spf R
for a
p
-torsionfree quasiregular semiperfectoid ring
R
.
For this, choose a perfectoid ring
R_{0}
mapping to
R
; then also the initial object
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R_{0}},(\xi))
of the absolute prismatic site of
R_{0}
maps to the initial object
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R},I)
of the absolute prismatic site of
R
and, in particular, we conclude that
I=\xi{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
is principal by rigidity. Now recall that
R^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\Spf\Rees(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{N}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})/\mathbb{G}_{m}
by
Example

2.3.13
and that
the
F
-gauge
E
arises by endowing the module
M
of global sections of a vector bundle on
\Spec({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})\setminus V(p,I)
equipped with a Frobenius structure
\tau:\varphi^{*}M[1/I]\cong M[1/I]
with its saturated Nygaardian filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}M

by
Proposition

5.2.6
.
We begin by noting that
M
is
p
-torsionfree since
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
is and therefore the pullback
\Fil^{\bullet}M/p
of
E
to
(R^{\mathrm{N}})_{p=0}
is concentrated in degree zero. Now further observe that
M/I
is
p
-torsionfree by virtue of the exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M[1/I]\oplus M[1/p]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M[1/pI]\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;;}}

indeed, if
pm=am^{\prime}
for some
m,m^{\prime}\in M
and
a\in I
, then we would have
m/a=m^{\prime}/p
, i.e.
m/a\in M
, which would in turn imply that
m
is already zero in
M/I
. This now
implies that,
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the map

\Fil^{i}M/p\rightarrow\Fil^{i-1}M/p
is injective: If
m=pm^{\prime}
for some
m\in\Fil^{i}M
and
m^{\prime}\in\Fil^{i-1}M
, then
p\tau(m^{\prime})=\tau(m)\in I^{i}M
;
as
\tau(m^{\prime})\in I^{i-1}
and
I
is generated by
\xi
, we obtain the equation
p\tfrac{\tau(m^{\prime})}{\xi^{i-1}}=\tfrac{\tau(m)}{\xi^{i-1}}\in IM
and and hence
\tfrac{\tau(m^{\prime})}{\xi^{i-1}}\in IM
, i.e.
\tau(m^{\prime})\in I^{i}M
,
as
M/I
is
p
-torsionfree – however, this implies
m^{\prime}\in\Fil^{i}M
by definition of the saturated Nygaardian filtration and hence already
m=0\in\Fil^{i}M/p
. Therefore, also the pullback
\gr^{\bullet}M/p=(\Fil^{\bullet}M/p)/(\Fil^{\bullet+1}M/p)
of
E
to
(R^{\mathrm{N}})_{p=t=0}=R^{\mathrm{N}}_{\mathrm{HT},c}
is concentrated in degree zero.

Finally, to compute the further pullback of
E
to
R^{\mathrm{N}}_{\mathrm{Hod}}
, observe that there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\mathrm{N}}_{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{0,\mathrm{dR},+}^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{0}^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

in which the large rectangle and the bottom square are pullbacks by definition. Thus, also the top square is a pullback and recalling that
R_{0,\mathrm{dR},+}=(R_{0}^{\mathrm{N}})_{p=u^{p}=0}
, where we use the isomorphism

R_{0}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\Spf(A_{\mathrm{inf}}(R_{0})\langle u,t\rangle/(ut-\varphi^{-1}(\xi)))/\mathbb{G}_{m}

from
Example

2.3.13
, we learn that
R^{\mathrm{N}}_{\mathrm{dR},+}
identifies with the locus

\{p=\varphi^{-1}(\xi)^{p}t^{-p}=0\}=\{p=\xi t^{-p}=0\}\subseteq R^{\mathrm{N}}\;.

Thus, we conclude that the pullback of
\gr^{\bullet}M/p
from
R^{\mathrm{N}}_{\mathrm{HT},c}
to
R^{\mathrm{N}}_{\mathrm{Hod}}=R^{\mathrm{N}}_{\mathrm{HT},c}\cap R^{\mathrm{N}}_{\mathrm{dR},+}
identifies with the graded object given by
the cofibre of the map

(\Fil^{\bullet-p}M/p)/(\Fil^{\bullet-p+1}M/p)\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}/p}\xi{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}/p\rightarrow(\Fil^{\bullet}M/p)/(\Fil^{\bullet+1}M/p)\;.

However,
recalling that
I=\xi{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
,
by definition of the saturated Nygaardian filtration, this is clearly an injection since multiplication by
\xi
corresponds to multiplication by
\xi^{p}
after applying
\tau
by
\varphi
-linearity
and
\xi
is a non-zerodivisor.

∎

Proof of
Theorem

5.0.1
.

As observed previously,
Theorem

5.2.7
reduces us to having the conclusion of
Proposition

5.3.1
for reflexive
F
-gauges instead of just vector bundle
F
-gauges. However, note
that all we really need
in order
to copy the proofs of
Proposition

5.3.1
and
Theorem

4.0.2
verbatim for a reflexive
F
-gauge
E
is that the pullback of
E
to
(X^{\mathrm{Hod}})_{p=0}
is concentrated in cohomological degree zero.
As this follows from
Lemma

5.3.3
and the flatness of the cover
(X^{\mathrm{Hod}})_{p=0}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}_{\mathrm{Hod}}
from (
4.2.4
), we are done.

∎

Appendix A
Some base change results

Throughout this paper, we often use base change results for cartesian squares of formal stacks like

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}}

However, as we are working with formal stacks instead of algebraic (or derived) stacks, such base change compatibilities are usually not automatic. Thus, in this appendix, we indicate how to prove these statements – in the main body of the paper, we will then often use them without further mention.

Definition A.0.1
.

We say that a cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

of stacks satisfies
base change for bounded below complexes
if, for any
F\in\mathcal{D}^{+}(X)
, see
Definition

3.4.1
,
there is an isomorphism

g^{*}f_{*}F\cong f^{\prime}_{*}{g^{\prime}}^{*}F

given by the natural map.

As it turns out,
many
of the base change results we need are an application of the following general statement:

Proposition A.0.2
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be a morphism of stacks over
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
such that
\mathcal{X}
can be written as the colimit of a simplicial flat affine formal
\mathcal{Y}
-scheme
U^{\bullet}
and assume that
\mathcal{Y}
admits a flat cover from a regular affine formal scheme.
Then,
for any locally closed immersion
i:\mathcal{Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
, the cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

satisfies base change for bounded below complexes.

Before we can prove the above proposition, we need to establish the following technical statement about the commutation of filtered colimits with cosimplicial totalisations:

Lemma A.0.3
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a stack admitting a flat cover from an affine scheme or a noetherian affine formal scheme. Assume we are given a filtered diagram of cosimplicial objects

F:I\times\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})

in the category
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
whose terms are all cosimplicial objects of the full subcategory
\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{X})
, i.e.
I
is a filtered category and
\Delta
denotes the simplex category, as usual. Then taking colimits over
I
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
commutes with cosimplicial totalisation, i.e. there is a natural isomorphism

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}\Tot(F(i,\bullet))\cong\Tot(\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}F(i,\bullet))}

in the category
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
.

Proof.

We first claim that filtered colimits in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
have finite cohomological dimension, i.e. that there is some
r\geq 0
with the property that, if
G,G^{\prime}:K\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
are two filtered diagrams of the same shape together with a natural transformation
G\rightarrow G^{\prime}
with the property that
\tau^{\leq-1}G(k)\cong\tau^{\leq-1}G^{\prime}(k)
for all
k\in K
, then there is a natural isomorphism

(A.0.1)

\tau^{\leq-r-1}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{k\in K}G(k)\cong\tau^{\leq-r-1}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{k\in K}G^{\prime}(k)\;.

Indeed, as colimits commute with pullbacks, this may be checked on a flat cover. Now there are two cases to consider: If we are given a flat cover
\Spec A\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
, the claim is clear as filtered colimits of
A
-modules are exact. If, however, we are given a flat cover
\Spf A\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
with
A
noetherian, then colimits in
\mathcal{D}(\Spf A)\cong\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(A)
are computed by first taking the colimit in
\mathcal{D}(A)
and then applying derived completion, so in this case the claim follows from the fact that derived completion has finite cohomological dimension in our setting due to the ideal of definition of
A
being finitely generated, see
[
41
, Tag 0AAJ]
. Thus, the claim is proved. In particular, note that, by picking
G^{\prime}(k)=0
for all
k
, we have also shown that, if
G(k)\in\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{X})
for all
k\in K
, then

\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{k\in K}G(k)\in\mathcal{D}^{\geq-r}(\mathcal{X})\;.

We now prove the claim from the statement. To check that the natural map

\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}\Tot(F(i,\bullet))\rightarrow\Tot(\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}F(i,\bullet))

is an isomorphism, it suffices to check that it induces an isomorphism on
\tau^{\leq n}
for all
n
. As
\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}F(i,\bullet)\in\mathcal{D}^{\geq-r}(\mathcal{X})
by the first paragraph, the totalisation on the right-hand side may be replaced by a partial totalisation if we are only interested in the result after
n
-truncation; more precisely, we have

\tau^{\leq n}\Tot(\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}F(i,\bullet))\cong\tau^{\leq n}\lim_{j\in\Delta_{\leq n+r+1}}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}F(i,j)\;,

where
\Delta_{\leq n+r+1}
denotes the full subcategory of
\Delta
spanned by the objects
j
with
j\leq n+r+1
. Now using that finite limits commute with filtered colimits and the finite cohomological dimension of filtered colimits, we obtain

\begin{split}\tau^{\leq n}\lim_{j\in\Delta_{\leq n+r+1}}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}F(i,j)&\cong\tau^{\leq n}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}\lim_{j\in\Delta_{\leq n+r+1}}F(i,j)\\
&\cong\tau^{\leq n}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}\tau^{\leq n+r}\lim_{j\in\Delta_{\leq n+r+1}}F(i,j)\;.\end{split}

Finally, we use that
F(i,j)\in\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{X})
to conclude that the
(n+r)
-truncation of the partial totalisation above actually agrees with the
(n+r)
-truncation of the full totalisation and make use of the finite cohomological dimension of filtered colimits once again to conclude

\begin{split}\tau^{\leq n}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}\tau^{\leq n+r}\lim_{j\in\Delta_{\leq n+r+1}}F(i,j)&\cong\tau^{\leq n}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}\tau^{\leq n+r}\Tot(F(i,\bullet))\\
&\cong\tau^{\leq n}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}\Tot(F(i,\bullet))\;,\end{split}

so we are done.

∎

Proof of
Proposition

A.0.2
.

We follow the argument given in the proof of
[
6
, Thm. 3.3.5]
. Namely, using the presentations
U^{\bullet}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
and
U^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{Z}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{Z}}
, we are reduced to checking that the formation of cosimplicial totalisations in
\mathcal{D}^{\geq n}(\mathcal{Y})
commutes with pullback along
i
. However, as
i
is a locally closed immersion, pullback along
i
is merely tensoring with
i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}}
and this is a perfect complex over some localisation of
\mathcal{Y}
by regularity of
\mathcal{Y}
. Thus, the claim now follows from the fact that cosimplicial totalisations in
\mathcal{D}^{\geq n}(\mathcal{Y})
commute with filtered colimits,
see
Lemma

A.0.3
,
as well as tensoring with perfect complexes.
∎

By way of example, we will now indicate how to use
Proposition

A.0.2
to prove many of the base change results we need.

Corollary A.0.4
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme which is
p
-quasisyntomic and qcqs. Then the cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}}

satisfies base change for bounded below complexes.

Proof.

To apply
Proposition

A.0.2
, first
recall
that
j_{\mathrm{dR}}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is an open immersion and that
there is a flat cover

\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle u,t\rangle\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
,
see
Remark

2.3.14
. To provide the necessary simplicial presentation of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
, first observe that, by quasisyntomic descent, see
Lemma

2.3.20
, we may assume that
X=\Spf R
for a quasiregular semiperfectoid ring
R
. However, then the claim is clear by the explicit description of
R^{\mathrm{N}}
from
Example

2.3.13
.
∎

Corollary A.0.5
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme which is smooth and qcqs. Then the cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Hod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Hod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+}}

satisfies base change for bounded below complexes.

Proof.

As
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+}=\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}

admits a flat cover from the regular affine formal scheme
\mathbb{A}^{1}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Hod}}=B\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is a closed immersion, we again want to apply
Proposition

A.0.2
. To obtain the necessary simplicial presentation of
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
, first observe that the functor
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
preserves (affine) étale maps by the proof of
[
6
, Thm. 2.3.6]
. As
X
Zariski-locally admits an affine étale map
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}
, we may thus assume that we are given an affine étale map
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{n})^{\mathrm{dR},+}=(\mathbb{G}_{a}/\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp})^{n}
of stacks over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
Then the pullback
\widetilde{X}
of the
{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}}^{n}
-torsor
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{n}\rightarrow(\mathbb{G}_{a}/\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp})^{n}
to
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is a flat affine formal scheme over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
and we have
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\cong\widetilde{X}/{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}}^{n}
, which yields the desired presentation.

∎

The second strategy we can employ to prove base change results is to explicitly identify descent data. Let us again explain what we mean by this using an example:

Lemma A.0.6
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme which is
p
-quasisyntomic and qcqs. Then the cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{N}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X^{\mathrm{N}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{N}}}

satisfies base change for bounded below complexes.

Proof.

Given any
F\in\mathcal{D}^{+}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
,
by base change for the cartesian diagrams

\lxSVG@closescope {}{
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}
{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.39998pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -12.85 -23.72 L 20.31 -23.72}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{14.87645pt}{-17.14146pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 20.58 -23.72)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-4.05957pt}{-13.42761pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -5.62 -18.58)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\;,

see
Corollary

A.0.4
,
we have

j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{*}\pi_{X^{\mathrm{N}},*}j_{\mathrm{N}}^{*}F\cong\pi_{X{\Delta},*}j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{*}j_{\mathrm{N}}^{*}F\cong\pi_{X{\Delta},*}j_{\mathrm{HT}}^{*}j_{\mathrm{N}}^{*}F\cong j_{\mathrm{HT}}^{*}\pi_{X^{\mathrm{N}},*}j_{\mathrm{N}}^{*}F\;,

where
j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{*}j_{\mathrm{N}}^{*}F\cong j_{\mathrm{HT}}^{*}j_{\mathrm{N}}^{*}F
is due to the fact that
j_{\mathrm{N}}^{*}F
is pulled back from
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
. Thus,
we see that
\pi_{X^{\mathrm{N}},*}j_{\mathrm{N}}^{*}F
is equipped with a canonical descent datum to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and this descent datum corresponds to the complex
\pi_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}},*}F
.
∎

The only base change claim which cannot be readily proved with either of the two techniques above is the following:

Lemma A.0.7
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme which is smooth and qcqs. Then the cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{dR},+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X^{\mathrm{N}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{dR},+}}

satisfies base change for bounded below complexes.

Proof.

To check that the base change map
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}^{*}\pi_{X^{\mathrm{N}},*}F\rightarrow\pi_{X^{\mathrm{dR},+},*}i_{\mathrm{dR},+}^{*}F
is an isomorphism for any
F\in\mathcal{D}^{+}(X^{\mathrm{N}})
, we may reduce mod
p
by
p
-completeness, i.e. we may pull back to the stack
(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})_{p=0}
. Moreover, as the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})_{p=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})_{p=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}

satisfies base change for bounded below complexes by the proof of
Corollary

A.0.5
, we are reduced to proving that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})_{p=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}}

satisfies base change for bounded below complexes; note that we have now simply written
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
instead of
(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})_{p=0}
as any instance of the stack
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
will be over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
from now on. However, note that the above square admits a factorisation as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})_{p=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}_{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

so it suffices to show that both the left and the right square satisfy base change for bounded below complexes. For the right square, as
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is a closed immersion
by the description of the reduced locus
, this follows from an application of
Proposition

A.0.2
; for the left square, we recall that
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}
and hence also
(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})_{p=0}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}_{\mathrm{dR},+}
is a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor, where
\mathcal{G}
is the group scheme described at the end of Section
2.4
, so here the claim follows by identifying descent data.
∎

Appendix B
Finiteness of pushforwards along
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}

In this appendix, we will prove a finiteness result for pushforwards of perfect complexes along
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
. Namely, the result we want to
establish
goes as follows:

Proposition B.0.1
.

Let
X
be a
p
-adic formal scheme which is smooth and proper
over
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. For any perfect
F
-gauge
E\in\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
, the pushforward
\pi_{X^{\mathrm{Syn}},*}E
is perfect.

Before we can proceed to the proof, we first need to establish the following two lemmas:

Lemma B.0.2
.

We work over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.

(i)

A complex
E\in\mathcal{D}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
is perfect if and only if it corresponds to a graded complex consisting of only finitely many nonzero graded pieces, which are all bounded complexes of finite-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-vector spaces.

(ii)

For a complex
E\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

E
is perfect.

(2)

E
is
t
-complete and its pullback to
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
is perfect (recall that
t
denotes the coordinate on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
).

(3)

Under the Rees equivalence,
E
corresponds to a finite filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}V
such that all graded pieces
\gr^{\bullet}V
are bounded complexes of finite-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-vector spaces; here, the filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}V
being finite means that
\Fil^{\bullet}V=0
for
\bullet\gg 0
and that the transition maps
\Fil^{\bullet}V\rightarrow\Fil^{\bullet-1}V
are isomorphisms for
\bullet\ll 0
.

Proof.

Part (i) is clear as perfectness may be checked after pullback along
\Spec\mathbb{F}_{p}\rightarrow B\mathbb{G}_{m}
. For part (ii), first observe that, if
E\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
is perfect, then its pullback to
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
must be perfect as well and
[
6
, Rem. 2.2.7]
shows that
E
is
t
-complete; hence, (1)
\Rightarrow
(2). The implication (2)
\Rightarrow
(3) is clear by (i) as the Rees equivalence matches
t
-completeness with completeness as a filtered object. Finally, to prove (3)
\Rightarrow
(1), note that (3) ensures that the pullback of
E
to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
is a bounded complex of finitely generated
\mathbb{F}_{p}[t]
-modules – however, as
\mathbb{F}_{p}[t]
is regular, this is the same as being perfect.

∎

Lemma B.0.3
.

Let
X
be a smooth qcqs
p
-adic formal scheme. Then for any perfect complex
E\in\Perf((X^{\mathrm{dR},+})_{p=0})
on
(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})_{p=0}
, the pushforward
\pi_{X^{\mathrm{dR},+},*}E\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
is
t
-complete (where we recall that
t
denotes the coordinate on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
).

Proof.

We employ a similar strategy to the proof of
[
6
, Thm. 2.3.6]
: One first observes that if
X
is written as a colimit of a finite diagram
U^{\bullet}
of affine open subschemes, then
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is the colimit of the diagram
U^{\bullet,\mathrm{dR},+}
and thus, by smoothness and the qcqs assumption, we may assume that
X=\Spf R
is affine and that there is an étale map
\Spf R\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}
. Then the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spf R\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{n}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{n})^{\mathrm{dR},+}}

is cartesian and the bottom map is a torsor for the group scheme
{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}}^{n}
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, hence the same is true for the top row. This means that the map
(R^{\mathrm{dR},+})_{p=0}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
admits a factorisation

(B.0.1)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(R^{\mathrm{dR},+})_{p=0}\cong(\Spec R/p\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})/{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}}^{n}\rightarrow B_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}(1))^{\sharp}}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}

and hence the pushforward of a quasi-coherent complex along this map admits a similar description to the one in
Lemma

4.2.9
: Namely, we can identify a quasi-coherent complex
E
on
(R^{\mathrm{dR},+})_{p=0}
with a complex
V
of
R/p
-modules equipped with a decreasing filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}V
and a flat connection
\nabla:V\rightarrow V\otimes\Omega_{(R/p)/\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{1}
having locally nilpotent
p
-curvature such that the restriction of
\nabla
to
\Fil^{i}V
comes with a factorisation through
\Fil^{i-1}V\otimes\Omega_{(R/p)/\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{1}
along the lines of
Remark

2.2.12
. Now observing that
\Omega_{(R/p)/\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{1}\cong(R/p)^{n}
since
\Spf R
is étale over
\mathbb{A}^{n}
and using the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant version of
Lemma

4.2.9
, we find that
\pi_{R^{\mathrm{dR},+},*}E
identifies with the filtered complex whose degree
i
term is given by

(B.0.2)

\Tot(\Fil^{i}V\xrightarrow{\nabla}\Fil^{i-1}V\otimes\Omega_{(R/p)/\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{1}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\Fil^{i-2}V\otimes\Omega_{(R/p)/\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{2}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\dots)

by virtue of the factorisation (
B.0.1
).
However, if
E
is perfect, then the filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}V
is finite by
Lemma

B.0.2
and hence also the filtration given by (
B.0.2
) is finite and, in particular, complete, so we are done as the Rees equivalence matches completeness as a filtered object with
t
-completeness.

∎

Proof of
Proposition

B.0.1
.

Recalling from the proof of
Proposition

4.1.3
that
v_{1}
is topologically nilpotent on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
, we may check perfectness after pulling back to the reduced locus
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
by
(p,v_{1})
-completeness; as the natural map
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is a cover, we may even pull back to
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{N}}
. By the gluing description of
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{red}}^{\mathrm{N}}
, we first observe that the reasoning of
[
6
, Fn. 44]
shows that it suffices to check perfectness after pullback to
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT},c}^{\mathrm{N}}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}^{\mathrm{N}}
. Finally, recall that there are covers
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{dR},+}
and
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathrm{HT},c}
, so we may check perfectness after pullback along those.

Using
Lemma

B.0.2
and
some base change results analogous to the ones from Appendix
A
, we are thus reduced to showing the following assertions:

(a)

Pushforward along
(X^{\mathrm{Hod}})_{p=0}\rightarrow B\mathbb{G}_{m}
preserves perfect complexes;

(b)

pushforward along
(X^{\not{D},c})_{p=0}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
carries perfect complexes to
t
-complete objects;

(c)

pushforward along
(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})_{p=0}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
carries perfect complexes to
t
-complete objects.

For (a), we observe that this follows immediately from
Lemma

B.0.2
together with
Proposition

4.2.11
; here, we make use of the properness assumption in order to conclude that cohomology on
X_{p=0}
preserves perfect complexes. Moreover, (b) follows immediately by the same reasoning as in the proof of
[
27
, Lem. 3.3.4]
. Finally, (c) is exactly
Lemma

B.0.3
.
∎
